## Faster near-parabolic truncation repair

The strict static-lowering trace exposed a regular full-rank hyperboloid whose
two truncation loops were not represented before the old radius limit. The
captured quadric is near the paraboloid boundary but is not exactly rank
deficient. A direct triangulator probe showed that subdivision levels 2–6 did
not help at the old limit; allowing 16 radius doublings produced two loops at
radius 4096 (the former limit reached only 2048). Accordingly,
`QuadricTriangulationSettings.maximumRadiusDoublings` now defaults to 16.
`quadric-triangulator-regression` now audits this exact captured quadric for
two loops, interface/cap residuals, and a strict closed positive boundary; it
passed.

With that fix, the same frozen deterministic static-lowering trace moved past
the old truncation failure and reduced residual from `1.19e-4` to
`4.88e-7` within the unchanged 60-evaluation cap. It then approached a
rank-deficient parabolic-cylinder stratum whose full-rank solver correctly
reported the six-dimensional fixed-stratum derivative as unavailable. Thus
the triangulation bug is fixed, but strict static-lowering end-to-end
convergence remains unverified and is now specifically blocked on the
rank-stratum transition/differential, not on truncation radius. No ten-column
Jacobian was fabricated and no final material was granted.

## Static lower-COM and Pareto plane application — 2026-09-25

The paper's Section 4.2 / Eq. (13) gives a dedicated plane KKT condition for
lowering the center of mass. The specialized static solver now has a separate
four-coefficient plane-family path for that objective and the complete static
Pareto family. It solves the two horizontal first-moment constraints plus the
paper's stationarity relation on the normalized plane-coefficient sphere. The
coefficient differential remains 10-by-4; this is not padded into a generic
10-dimensional Eq. (14) solve. The exact Pareto endpoints dispatch to the
vertical maximum-mass path or the lowering path, respectively.

The plane evaluator now returns the actual variable-material boundary from
the same native Boolean operation as its moments and derivative. The boundary
is included in the scientific identity. Independent triangle-mesh integration
matched all ten moments within 1e-10 on the analytic plane fixture.

Validation in `build/strict-algorithms-exact-current-20260922`:

- `plane-family-regression`: analytic slice moments, all 40 plane derivatives,
  six multi-step finite-difference cases, target-frame transform, contact
  fail-closed behavior, and independent output-mesh integration passed.
- `plane-static-balance-regression`: maximum-mass, lower-COM, Pareto kappa=0,
  kappa=1, and one interior kappa case passed on a synthetic centered-shell
  box. The lower-COM cut matched the analytic value `-0.0333705`; normalized
  horizontal moments were below 2e-10 and the reported KKT residual was below
  8e-12. Iteration exhaustion and reversed half-space orientation were rejected
  rather than promoted to success.
- Two independent process runs produced identical scientific result lines.
- `faster-static-stability-contract-regression` and the strict off-target yoyo
  regression passed. The latter retained output hash
  `2aedd3ae0e43f562079c891b139d5877a699e7cbee77a33f274b75c18b4b6138`.
- Source-surface dual-density and independent material regressions passed;
  the reconstructed Sumone case still has zero retained dense cells, and its
  asymmetric optimizer control remains an expected failure.

This is synthetic method validation, not an author case or a complete static
application artifact: the request still represents fixed shell content by its
ten moments rather than a sealed fixed-shell mesh, and this result makes no
manufacturing-clearance claim. The general full-rank Eq. (14) lowering solve
remains blocked at the parabolic-cylinder stratum; this specialized
static-plane application does not close the general rank-stratum solver gap.
Source-cage 26-handle `TopMassAndLowering` remains `RESOURCE_LIMIT / INCOMPLETE`
and was not repeated without a geometry-guard performance diagnosis.

## Exact Cherchi planar-boundary follow-up — 2026-09-25

The Cherchi Level-7 captured trial was re-audited from its retained exact
construction sidecar rather than from rounded OBJ coordinates alone. For
`material-013.obj`, the 1,382-vertex planar output boundary matches the unique
1,380-segment exact source-plane section, with exactly two additional
collinear subdivisions. The section is simple. The actual Boolean operation
was recomputed from the frozen coefficients and closed source with the native
center-side selector: it is `intersection`, not `subtraction`. Exact parity
witnesses classify the loop interior as inside the source and a point in its
face complement as outside; the supported patch is therefore
`WITNESS_SUPPORTED_INTERIOR_DISK`. The earlier subtraction/`FACE_COMPLEMENT`
interpretation in this note was caused by assuming an operation that the failed
evaluation record had not saved; that interpretation is superseded.

The retained source `source-013.obj` was run through the independent CGAL
exact-predicate closed-mesh self-intersection checker: 61,110 vertices,
122,216 faces, `INTERSECTION_FREE`, and zero intersecting face pairs. This
supports the source parity premise for that trial; it does not prove global
Boolean equivalence or validate the raw Boolean output.

For this captured `intersection` disk, a diagnostic fan-cap candidate was
constructed only after requiring the exact patch witness, an independently
intersection-free source, a single orientable boundary cycle on the proven
plane, and a rounded kernel point. The boundary vertices were restored from
the exact sidecar before construction: 842 coordinates changed only by
roundoff (maximum displacement `9.99224815253781e-16`). The candidate is
closed edge-manifold, has 116,184 faces, and passed the exact CGAL
self-intersection audit with zero intersecting face pairs. An independent
signed-tetrahedron integration check agrees with all ten native moments to
normalized error `5.273559366969494e-16`. Its OBJ SHA-256 is
`b310636d782ef4ea77fa72e9289064031889b60e3b3ac81e2b72184c0a19a217`.
These checks validate the candidate mesh's internal properties, not that it is
the exact Cherchi Boolean result; `boolean_equivalence_proven=false`, and the
candidate is not a solver output.

The native CGAL evaluator could not provide an independent comparison: it
failed with `BOOLEAN_ARRANGEMENT_PWN_FAILURE` (`Input mesh is not PWN!`). A
separate exact Nef region comparison of the cap candidate against the intended
intersection timed out after 240 seconds under the 24-GiB job envelope (the
observed process RSS before timeout was about 6.9 GiB); this is no equivalence
result. The remaining blocker is proving candidate/Boolean entity equivalence
or obtaining a valid independent Boolean result, then verifying the resulting
boundary and derivatives in the Newton callback. Even that would not establish
Eq. (14) convergence or close the broader reference-Boolean integration.

Failure evidence now records the Boolean operation whenever source selection
completed before the failure. If source construction fails earlier, operation
remains absent rather than guessed. The captured Level-7 Newton result remains
failed and no cap candidate enters the production solver path.

Verification for this correction: the focused Faster Cherchi/Newton Python
runner passes 31/31 tests, `py_compile` succeeds, and the exact
self-intersection CTest passes 1/1. These are diagnostic/infrastructure checks;
they do not resolve the captured Boolean-equivalence blocker.

### Cherchi construction motion and actual Boolean moment differential — 2026-09-25

construction sidecar. It accepts only unique exact input-vertex mappings,

kernel differentiates the ten moments of the actual oriented Boolean output.

finite-difference audit for the synthetic ellipsoid and one-sheet hyperboloid:
the full nine-dimensional tangent space of normalized quadric coefficients,
three centered-difference scales, both signs, and a provenance-labeled
combinatorial topology check at each perturbation. It uses the exact-geometry

direction run with largest step `3e-4` was retained as
`build/strict-faster-motion-differential-exact-r4-20260925/`; its hyperboloid
coarse-step errors exceeded `1e-6` while shrinking by approximately nine when

The follow-up step set (`1e-4`, `3e-5`, `1e-5`) was fixed before rerun. Results
in `build/strict-faster-motion-differential-exact-r5-20260925/`:

  matrix remains discrepant for plane, paraboloid, and both hyperboloids, so

### External Cherchi map in the Eq.14 Newton loop — 2026-09-25

The former “not integrated” gap is now partially closed. A strict Python/native

moments, its matching 10x10 coefficient Jacobian, a derivative-validity flag,

restart counts, failure events, and geometry/topology identities are returned.
The API is explicitly numerical-only and cannot mark or seal final geometry.
The historical default evaluator path is unchanged.

run `build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-integration-20260925-r3/`, all three
Newton function evaluations performed real Cherchi Booleans. Evaluation 1
changed topology, reduced residual, and was accepted with fresh relinearization
on the next iteration. Evaluation 2 changed topology but did not reduce the
independently evaluated residual and was rejected. The preregistered 3-call
budget ended as `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` / `maximum_evaluations_reached`;
this is not host resource exhaustion, convergence, or an infeasibility proof.
No final material constraint audit ran because the solver did not converge.

overwritten.

A full 60-evaluation, one-restart synthetic attempt was then performed with
the current default quadric tessellation resolution (3) in
`build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-integration-20260925-r11/`. Its loop no
longer invokes CGAL: an initial-only CGAL preflight checked the frozen
positive-definite ellipsoid branch; each in-loop map used Cherchi output
moments and source-motion/Reynolds Jacobian. Of 60 solver evaluator calls, 29

Reynolds Jacobian, so the callback failed closed; the configured evaluation

The returned `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` token denotes this configured
evaluator-count cap, not host resource exhaustion or proven infeasibility. Its
recorded native-extension hash matches the extension used for the final CTest.
The otherwise similar r10 run has a different extension hash and is retained

The earlier low-resolution level-2 failure was independently replayed from its

This is one real, bounded synthetic solver integration, not a nontrivial
converged Faster result. LNC motion, some Cherchi/CGAL exact-solid discrepancies,

### Captured cap-loop regression follow-up — 2026-09-25

The r14 evaluation-59 coefficient vector is now a direct C++ triangulator
regression. With the frozen level-3 icosphere, automatic domain radius, and 16
bounded radius doublings, it still fails closed. The concrete final witness
is a boundary vertex with two directed outgoing edges and zero incoming edges;
the exception now reports both directed degrees and the target indices. A
level-4 direct probe retained the same degree defect at a different
deterministic vertex.

An independent diagnostic reconstructed each fixed normal-sphere level from
the same level-0 icosphere before measuring the inverse-Gauss vertex mask
(avoiding cumulative refinement between levels). At level 3, 32 finite
admissible vertices form 16 disconnected components, unchanged by the radius
doublings. At level 4, the sampled finite mask is fragmented too: it has 64
vertices/32 components at the initial radius and 94 vertices/2 components
after 16 doublings. This indicates that levels 3–4 under-resolve the
admissible normal domain for this coefficient vector; it does not prove a
continuous-domain failure or a global solver defect.

A candidate face-adjacency reorientation was tested and reverted. It changed
the established Heart low-resolution negative-control witness and yielded 16
boundary loops for the captured level-3 hyperboloid, not the required two.
No orientation rewrite or tolerance change is treated as a repair. A new
level-5 method regression, using the same fixed geometry and only four radius
doublings, produces the required two loops, passes closed/outward boundary,
quadric and cap-radius residual checks, and repeats to the same triangulation
hash. This is evidence that the case is resolvable with a finer declared
normal-sphere discretization; it does not justify silently changing the r14
level-3 profile or treating its failed callbacks as successful. The regression
retains both facts: level 3 may succeed only with a valid audit or explicitly
rejects; level 5 must pass the independent checks.

The targeted `spin-faster-quadric-triangulator` CTest passes, including the
pre-existing Heart low-resolution negative control. The captured Newton case
at the level-3 r14 profile remains an unresolved geometry callback and still
has no final-material verification. Level-5 triangulation is a module-level
geometric method result, not a full Newton convergence result.

### Joint-material budget and exact geometry-guard profile — 2026-09-25

The frozen 26-handle / 8-fill-variable `--joint-material` run r2 used the
registered four-stage, 120-iteration-per-stage, 24-line-search configuration
and the 24-GiB memory cap. Its supervisor terminated at the 60-minute
wall-clock limit (`Result=timeout`, exit 143), before the runner emitted a
completed solver trace or final material audit. The durable session log
contains only preflight and start records. This is `RESOURCE_LIMIT`, not a
convergence result, geometric infeasibility result, or algorithmic failure;
the same configuration will not be blindly rerun.

A separately registered one-call geometry-guard profile completed in 72.6472
seconds with 496 primitives and 122,760 primitive pairs. The timer recorded
approximately 23.58 s in per-primitive exact region validation, 10.06 s in
primitive-subset checks, 4.67 s in outer-domain clearance, and 34.24 s in pair
relations (2,988 exact overlap checks; the remaining pairs received strict
AABB-separation witnesses). These measurements explain why the full adaptive
joint run exhausted its wall-clock budget. They do not justify skipping or
weakening any geometry check. The next Spin-It action is therefore gated on a
provable semantics-preserving acceleration of this guard; otherwise retain
this bounded resource blocker and proceed with Faster integration.

The failed Newton run's saved evaluation-60 coefficient vector was also
replayed against the native source-motion builder. At level 5 it deterministically
fails with the same bounded hyperboloid truncation witness: a boundary vertex
has two outgoing and zero incoming edges (targets 163 and 166). The native
source-motion API at that point explicitly rejected levels 6 and 7 because its
source-matching budget supported only levels 0–5. That was a software budget
limit, not evidence that finer geometry would fail. The following section
records the level-7 path after replacing the quadratic match scan. The level-5
and radius-doubling observations remain historical diagnostics only.

For that exact evaluation-60 vector, raising only the temporary diagnostic
radius-doubling allowance from 4 to 8, 12, and 16 reproduced the identical
directed-degree witness at vertex 165 every time (0.009–0.036 s per attempt).
The coefficient quadratic-form eigenvalues are approximately
`[-4.15215e-5, 5.71694e-1, 7.95634e-1]`: the trial has crossed from the
positive-definite ellipsoid chart into a one-sheet-hyperboloid signature. The
radius allowance is therefore not the cause. In the original ellipsoid-only
integration profile, the accepted state was followed by line-search trials
that left that chart, and the callback failed before a Cherchi Boolean was
attempted. The runner was subsequently extended to admit audited one-sheet and
two-sheet hyperboloid center-side branches; those changes and the follow-up
full runs are recorded below. The original failure remains historical
evidence, not a paper-case result or an infeasibility claim.

### Level-7 source-motion and captured Cherchi callback — 2026-09-25

The evaluation-60 coefficient vector has one small negative quadratic-form
eigenvalue (`-4.15e-5`), so its callback is on the one-sheet-hyperboloid side
of the ellipsoid transition. The direct triangulator succeeds at level 7,
although the CGAL evaluator later reports a separate PWN Boolean-arrangement
failure. To avoid conflating those stages, a source-motion regression was
added for this captured vector. It first failed as expected because
`evaluateQuadricSourceMotion` explicitly capped source matching at levels 0–5.

Root cause of that cap: original fixed-normal identity recovery scanned every
normal point for every interface vertex (`O(V_interface * V_normals)`). It was
replaced by a conservative spatial-bin index. The index only removes impossible
candidate pairs; every candidate still passes the original Euclidean tolerance,
and multiple matches still fail as ambiguous. The source-motion setting now
admits levels 0–7. After this change the new regression passes at level 7,
requiring two boundary loops and complete per-vertex provenance/Jacobian
dimensions.

At level 7, the captured trial source mesh has 4,033 vertices and 8,062 faces;
source-motion recovery took 39.48 s and produced truncation radius
`110.85125168440814`. The pinned Cherchi subtraction then completed, producing
4,192 material vertices and 8,384 faces. The closed-component orientation
audit reported one component and zero flips; all 4,192 construction records
were recoverable, with 20,824 unique operand references and no ambiguous or
unmatched references. The resulting construction-motion Jacobian is finite
and 10x10; the independently integrated raw mass is `5.588705176242677`.
The artifact is
`build/strict-faster-q60-level7-cherchi-probe-20260925-r2/completion.json`.

An independent centered finite-difference check used the normalized tangent
projection of coefficient direction `e0`. At steps `1e-5` and `1e-6`, the
Cherchi construction topology hash stayed identical and the relative errors
against the recovered 10x10 Jacobian were `1.20e-10` and `4.98e-10`, both
below `1e-6`. At `1e-4`, the positive perturbation changed source/material
mesh counts and produced degenerate Cherchi triangles, so it was rejected by

Newton convergence or final-material certification. The reproducible summary
is `build/strict-faster-q60-level7-cherchi-probe-20260925-r2/differential.json`.

A third fresh level-7 full run (`r3`) used the generalized full-rank

audit was attempted or claimed.

The captured two-sheet branch also exposed an important selector detail: the
Boolean side is not generally chosen from the algebraic quadric center. The
native implementation integrates the *current capped source boundary*,
computes that finite solid's centroid, evaluates the quadric there, then uses
the sign to select intersection versus subtraction. The runner now mirrors
that rule for every callback and rejects a near-zero selector; algebraic
signature checks are only a stratum preflight. A red/green unit test verifies
that a source-centroid sign differing from the algebraic-center sign controls
the operation. On the captured two-sheet source, the integrated centroid
selects `intersection` (`q(centroid)=+3.2121e-5`), matching the actual
Cherchi geometry artifact; the selector evidence is
`build/strict-faster-captured-two-sheet-probe-20260925/selector-audit.json`.
The fresh runner change passed its 8-test Python suite and registered CTest
1/1. The r3 full-run artifact predates this source-centroid selector change;
therefore its result is not evidence for a complete run of the updated runner.

A fourth full level-7 run (`r4`) used the updated source-centroid selector,
the fixed initial coefficients, one restart, the 60-evaluation budget, and a
180-second Cherchi timeout. It ended after 2,795.55 seconds with
`RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED`: all 60 evaluations were consumed, the normalized
residual remained `5.0771e-3`, and no final material was available for
independent audit. Sixteen accepted states were recorded. The last rejected
trial failed the required orientation/geometry check because its Cherchi
output contained a degenerate face; the runner did not use that geometry or
reuse its derivative. Event and trace hashes are
`eed737ea6603157a7f60ca57be1806de77c1e8868bbb77a7d71c91882dcdd417` and
`f59aad52ca4e289fa0226053512f47d940fbe0b9cd4530574e207497333b7356`.

The native Faster regression gates for discrete moments and end-to-end solver

### Exact source-surface self-intersection audit — 2026-09-25

The fast manufacturing self-intersection routine uses floating predicates and
therefore was not sufficient to admit a planar cap. Added a separate exact-
predicate CGAL audit that treats every binary64 coordinate as its exact
represented value, verifies the input is a closed edge-manifold triangle mesh,
orients the face soup without moving geometry or splitting vertices, and
enumerates intersecting triangle-face pairs. Unsupported topology returns an
UNKNOWN status; the audit never welds, drops, or creates geometry.

The RED test initially failed to compile because the audit API did not exist.
After implementation, its registered CTest passed on separated closed cubes,
crossing closed cubes (with a face-pair witness), and an open-shell rejection.
The exact audit was then run on retained `source-013.obj`: it returned
`INTERSECTION_FREE` for 61,110 vertices and 122,216 faces, with zero
intersecting face pairs. The source file SHA-256 is
`76827c5bbdae82fa0d9761c6dfa7370b5c3816a81878285fea8fa48eae165e11`.
CGAL successfully converted the oriented soup as a closed triangle mesh
without splitting vertices. This closes the source-self-intersection unknown
for this retained trial, but does not yet prove the output Boolean patch is
globally correct or produce an admitted cap; the next proof step is to compute
the complete exact plane section of the source and show it matches the output
boundary cycle before any material repair or moment integration.

### Planar Boolean face-region witness audit — 2026-09-25

Added an exact, read-only planar patch audit for open Boolean boundaries on a
design-domain face. It verifies that the output loop and design-face boundary
are simple, that the face boundary is one convex cycle, and that exact source
mesh parity at one loop-interior sample and one face-complement sample selects
the candidate Boolean patch for intersection or subtraction. The result is
explicitly `WITNESS_SUPPORTED_*`, not a global Boolean-equivalence proof; it
does not prove that the source has no self-intersections or that no additional
unobserved source-region changes occur across the design face. No cap is
generated.

On retained evaluation 13, the 1,382-vertex exact planar cycle on `y=-1` is
simple (60,226 candidate edge-pair checks, no crossings). The section centroid
has exact odd-parity source classification with one positive ray crossing;
the selected design-face complement witness `(-1,-1)` has even parity and no
crossings. For the recorded `intersection` branch this supports the candidate
`INTERIOR_DISK` patch. This isolates the likely missing planar patch but does
not admit or construct a capped solid: exact source self-intersection / solid
validity remains unknown, and the Cherchi and CGAL outputs remain unvalidated
as equivalent complete material objects.

A test also exposed and fixed `_failed_evaluation_record()` raising
`UnboundLocalError` when no artifact directory was supplied. The focused Python
runner tests pass 19/19 after both changes. This advances failure evidence and
localizes a face-region candidate; it does not change the Level-7 Newton result
or count as a successful Faster solve.

The failure-record path now also emits a non-mutating topology audit of any
retained raw Boolean OBJ. It reports edge-incidence class, boundary component
sizes, degree-two loop sizes, and approximate axis-aligned plane candidates;
these diagnostics do not close or validate the solid. A second audit binds
that boundary by binary64 memory-coordinate keys to the exact-rational
coordinate sidecar and to the face-label sidecar. A unit test deliberately
reorders exact-sidecar rows to ensure vertex IDs are not inferred from row
order. Both raw- and exact-boundary diagnostic tests pass in the focused Python
runner suite (14/14) after adding exact planar-cycle and orientation tests.

Applying the exact-boundary audit to evaluation 13 confirms one 1,382-vertex
cycle whose exact y-coordinate is `-1/1`; all 1,382 incident faces carry input
object mask `0x2`. An exact-rational grid broad phase and exact segment
predicates checked 60,226 nonadjacent candidate pairs and certified the loop
as a simple polygon with no crossings or touches. This proves only polygon
simplicity; it does not select the Boolean patch (interior versus complement)
or its orientation. The Boolean therefore remains `OPEN_BOUNDARY`, with no
synthetic cap, no accepted material moments, and no Newton success. The
Cherchi/CGAL planar-boundary discrepancy remains a blocking integration issue;
the next valid step is a separately tested arrangement/cap construction with
exact region and orientation checks, not a fan-cap or backend fallback.

The raw-incidence audit also now checks face-winding constraints without
changing faces. On evaluation 13 it finds 5,429 shared edges whose two output
faces use the same direction, but the face-adjacency parity system has no
orientation conflict; the 114,802-face open component is combinatorially
orientable, and a consistent orientation induces one directed 1,382-edge
boundary cycle. The diagnostic's arbitrary-root parity assignment flips
2,086 faces; that is only a witness, not an applied geometry repair. Therefore
the original boundary-normalizer failure combines open topology and locally
inconsistent winding, rather than a degenerate exact triangle. A valid adapter
would need to orient the open patch first, identify and verify the missing
design-face region, cap only that region, then run the existing closed-mesh
orientation and moment/derivative checks.

A one-off point-location diagnostic on the loop's exact shoelace centroid used
a `1e-12` padded floating projection-AABB broad phase followed by exact
rational ray/triangle predicates on the candidates. It found one positive-y
crossing and no boundary hit in the retained source mesh, consistent with the
centroid lying inside the source solid and with the observed `INTERSECTION`
branch. This is supporting evidence only: source self-intersection remains
unresolved, and the padded broad phase has not yet been promoted to a reusable
certified point-location routine. No cap was added from this result.

The same failed trial was executed through both probe modes: ordinary
`booleanPipeline` and diagnostic
`runBooleanPipelineRetainingArrangementPoints`. Both produced the same
58,093-vertex / 114,802-face OBJ byte-for-byte
(`788f00b3abf4f570b4339115ecadb6a115129125e2678e46d829e38277f0431e`) with

The source operand itself is closed (122,216 faces; every indexed edge has

The same failed trial was then executed through both probe modes: ordinary

`runBooleanPipelineRetainingArrangementPoints` path. Both produced the same
58,093-vertex / 114,802-face OBJ byte-for-byte
(`788f00b3abf4f570b4339115ecadb6a115129125e2678e46d829e38277f0431e`) with

#### Joint-adaptive geometry-guard cost probe

To localize the one-hour joint-adaptive resource limit without repeating the
full solve, the frozen 26-handle fixture was run through one standalone exact
geometry guard. It completed in `71.3113 s` on one CPU thread for 496
primitives and 122,760 primitive pairs. The checker retained 124,251 stage
records and performed 2,988 exact pair-overlap relations. Measured per-stage
totals were approximately 23.34 s for primitive-region audits, 9.91 s for
primitive-subset relations, 4.62 s for outer-domain clearance, and 33.35 s for
primitive-pair processing. This identifies repeated exact geometry validation
as a material runtime cost. The 400-call setting was derived from
retained-evidence memory capacity, not runtime feasibility; if every call cost
like this baseline probe, 400 checks would take roughly 7.9 hours before the
numerical/adaptation work. Candidate checks can be cheaper or more expensive,
so this is a planning bound, not an extrapolated run result. No guard-cache or
reduced-check optimization has been introduced: such a change needs an
equivalence-preserving certificate and a dedicated differential regression
before it can affect a strict run.

The fixed joint experiment therefore remains `RESOURCE_LIMIT` and will not be
retried as-is. Work advances to the Faster geometry-to-Newton integration
track. Any later guard optimization must first preserve the full geometry
witness semantics demonstrated by the current checker.

#### Faster failed-trial artifact binding

The Cherchi/Newton integration runner now attaches every artifact that exists
for a failed evaluation to that failure record: source triangulation, raw
Boolean OBJ, normalized OBJ, construction provenance, and exact/face
construction tables, each with a relative path and SHA-256. Failure stage is
derived from the last available pipeline artifact, so a Boolean result rejected
during orientation normalization is distinguished from a Boolean execution
failure or a later differential failure. The record continues to leave
`geometry_hash` and `topology_hash` null when no validated differentiable
geometry was produced; hashing raw output does not promote it to a valid
material result.

TDD verification: the new artifact-binding assertion first failed because the
runner did not accept an artifact directory, then passed after the minimal
implementation. The runner unit suite passed 9/9, and registered CTest
`spin-faster-cherchi-newton-runner-config` passed 1/1. This is an evidence
quality improvement only; it does not fix the observed sliver-face failures or
change Newton outcomes.

Follow-up on the same retained Level-7 failed trial (evaluation 13) separated
the two predicates that had previously been collapsed into one message. The
candidate face rejected by the floating area threshold has an exact nonzero
binary64-dyadic cross product (`2.206464731286154e-15` in norm). A new slow-path
exact predicate now accepts such a nonzero sliver while continuing to reject
exactly collinear vertices; it does not modify coordinates or faces. A closed
unit-cube fixture with a `1e-15` nonzero triangle verifies orientation
normalization and polyhedral moment derivatives at three finite-difference
steps. The focused CTest passed 1/1 after the change.

Applying that predicate to the retained Cherchi OBJ then exposed the actual
blocking topology: the raw Boolean mesh has 1,382 boundary edges, zero edges
with incidence greater than two, and all boundary edges lie on the design-box
plane `y=-1`; every boundary vertex has degree two. The checker still rejects
this geometry as open (no caps are synthesized). An independent CGAL evaluation
of the same recorded quadric coefficients also failed, with
`BOOLEAN_ARRANGEMENT_PWN_FAILURE` during winding-number propagation. Thus the
exact-sliver predicate improved diagnosis but did not turn the trial into a
valid material result. The retained artifact hashes are available by calling
the updated failure-record helper on evaluation 13 in
`build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-level7-failure-capture-20260925/`; its raw
OBJ SHA-256 is
`788f00b3abf4f570b4339115ecadb6a115129125e2678e46d829e38277f0431e` and the
source OBJ SHA-256 is
`76827c5bbdae82fa0d9761c6dfa7370b5c3816a81878285fea8fa48eae165e11`. The next
algorithm task is to isolate the planar boundary-cap arrangement case and
verify it against both backends before any capped output is admitted.

### Cherchi eval-13 orientation and same-map derivative follow-up — 2026-09-25

The retained evaluation-13 coefficients were read from that run's

quadric source had 10,287 same-direction shared edges despite being closed and
without exact self-intersections. `orientComponentsOutward` now first runs

sign from signed volume, and independently verifies piecewise-constant winding
number. The regression is registered as
`spin-faster-quadric-source-winding`; focused source-motion, stratum-tangent,
quadric-moment, and source-winding CTests passed 4/4 after rebuilding. This is
a source-orientation repair, not a Boolean or optimizer success.

With that corrected source, one pinned Cherchi `intersection` evaluation at the
retained q produced 58,093 vertices and 116,182 faces. The exact represented-
boundary audit completed: one closed edge-manifold component, consistent
winding, no degenerate faces, and no exact self-intersections. A separate
Python geometric-moment integrator agreed with the native ten raw moments at
normalized error `9.55719284207275e-16`. The evidence is in
`build/faster-cherchi-eval13-oriented-source-20260925/`, including the native
and independent moment vectors and hashes. This is a synthetic
single-evaluation result, not a sealed solver output.

Construction provenance recovered a finite `10x10` moment Jacobian for that

records (55,323 explicit vertices and 2,770 line-plane intersections; no
ambiguous or unmatched sources). Its digest and source-motion dimensions are

constructed at this individual q, but not that it differentiates the actual
Cherchi Boolean map on a neighborhood.

A fixed `e0` coefficient perturbation of `h=1e-6` exposed a real
provenance-labeled combinatorial branch change. Base / plus / minus outputs
had `(58,093, 116,182)`, `(58,074, 116,144)`, and `(58,111, 116,218)`
vertex/face counts, respectively, and three distinct construction-topology
signatures. Therefore a same-smooth-branch finite-difference comparison was
not computed and the derivative gate is explicitly recorded as failed / not
verified in
`build/faster-cherchi-eval13-oriented-source-20260925/finite-difference-q0/result.json`.
No Newton direction may reuse this Jacobian across the observed branch event.
The next algorithm work is to classify and handle Cherchi topology changes
(including relinearization or trial rejection) before attempting the full
Newton integration again. This is not an infeasibility certificate and does
not count as a Faster paper case.

### Full strict Newton follow-up after source-orientation repair — 2026-09-25

The repaired evaluator was exercised inside the full strict Eq. 14 external-

budget. All 60 callbacks returned geometry and a finite Jacobian; the run took
2,233.09 seconds. It accepted 18 residual-decreasing topology transitions
with fresh relinearization and rejected 41 topology-changing trials that did
not lower residual. The normalized equation residual fell from
`0.4154174643` to `0.0016956628` (about 245x), but remained far above
`1e-12`; the run exhausted its evaluation budget with status
`RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED`, diagnostic
`fixed-topology derivative guard rejected Boolean topology transition`, and
`final_geometry_verified=false`. Event and trace hashes are
`9b186f6970be66b8e41bd672f498c7643e9eb6222452d306590f18a647bf0d92` and
`7f7f5ba5979d7fb041e6b9ce395358096141d99e338abb17ba2bc370cb72cb52`.

The last accepted state is callback 57 (the terminal line-search probes were
not accepted). Its 2,641-vertex / 5,274-face output passed the independent
exact represented-boundary audit: closed, edge- and vertex-manifold,
consistently outward oriented, nondegenerate, and exact self-intersection-free.
Native and independent ten-component moment integration agreed at normalized
error `1.2168893991224345e-15`. The audit is stored in
`build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-oriented-source-20260925/terminal-accepted-state-independent-audit.json`.
It does not validate the design-domain relationship or turn the nonconverged
intermediate into a final result.

The attempted three-step `e0` finite-difference diagnostic stopped at its
first step (`h=1e-6`) because both perturbed Cherchi geometries changed
provenance-labeled topology from the base. Their vertex/face counts changed
from `58,093/116,182` to `58,074/116,144` and `58,111/116,218`.
Consequently no moment finite difference was compared and the `1e-6`
derivative gate is not passed. This result is preserved in
`build/faster-cherchi-eval13-oriented-source-20260925/finite-difference-q0/result.json`.

The `e0` perturbation above was only a branch-localization probe, not a
unit-sphere tangent update. A separate solver-coordinate diagnostic was run
at the last accepted Newton point: project `e0` into the coefficient-sphere
tangent, normalize it, and use geodesic `q(h)=cos(h)q ± sin(h)d` updates with
preregistered steps `1e-4`, `3e-5`, and `1e-5`. The first step already crossed
strata, so the protocol stopped without computing a moment difference. The
plus side changed Boolean operation from subtraction to intersection; the
minus side remained subtraction but changed provenance-labeled topology and
grew from 5,274 to 325,954 faces. Thus the finite Jacobian at the base point
does not pass a neighborhood-validity check. The result is recorded in
`build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-oriented-source-20260925/terminal-tangent-fd-20260925/result.json`.
This is a branch-boundary witness, not a derivative-error measurement on one
smooth stratum.

This batch closes one source-winding defect, demonstrates that the
topology-transition guard and fresh-relinearization path execute on actual
Cherchi geometries, and independently checks one last accepted intermediate.
It does **not** close Faster derivative-validity or Newton-convergence
requirements: finite differences within a fixed topology stratum remain
unverified, and the strict solve did not converge. No author case or
`VERIFIED_COMPUTATIONAL_REPRODUCTION` status is claimed.

### Selector-margin trace instrumentation — 2026-09-25

The branch-localization evidence showed that the capped-source centroid
selector can be much closer to its existing sign-transition guard than the
Newton trial scale. At the last accepted state its signed value was
`-3.6630820755e-7` (subtraction); the tangent `h=1e-4` positive probe changed
the value to `+9.9632873580e-5` (intersection), while the negative probe was
`-1.0036557025e-4` and also changed source triangulation complexity. The
numerical selector was therefore being recorded only as a categorical
operation, obscuring the branch margin in ordinary evaluation traces.

The strict runner now factors this unchanged rule into
`_closed_source_selector_details`. Each successful Boolean evaluation records
the closed-source centroid, quadratic value, scale, existing absolute
threshold, signed/normalized margin, and clearance to the threshold. The
compatibility operation helper delegates to the same calculation; branch
selection and tolerances are unchanged. Regression tests first failed because
the details API did not exist, then the initial full runner Python suite passed
`33/33`; Python byte-compilation and `git diff --check` also passed.

Near-threshold and nonfinite selector failures now carry the same computed
details into the failed-evaluation record instead of retaining only an error
string. A regression first failed because that evidence field was absent, then
the registered runner CTest passed `1/1` with `34/34` test methods. The
operation-bound topology identity test confirms that identical construction
provenance under intersection and subtraction yields distinct Newton strata.

The topology identity passed into the Newton fixed-stratum guard now also
includes the actual Boolean operation (`intersection` versus `subtraction`),
in addition to the construction-provenance/face-topology signature. This
closes a cache/stratum identity gap: two material maps can no longer share a
derivative identity merely because their emitted combinatorics happen to
match. Its regression was observed failing before the helper existed, then
passed. The registered Cherchi runner CTest passed `1/1`, and the broader
Faster Python API CTest passed `16/16`, including the actual discrete-Jacobian
Newton topology-transition/relinearization fixture. `py_compile` and
`git diff --check` passed.

The selector instrumentation and operation-bound topology identity improve
stratum evidence and prevent operation-mismatched Jacobian reuse, but they do
not repair the discrete derivative or establish local differentiability.
Because the evaluator identity changed, the prior 60-evaluation strict run is
historical evidence for its prior identity, not a validation of this revision.
No full Cherchi/Newton rerun was launched: host swap was already active, while
the registered expensive protocol requires swap=0. Faster's strict solve and
algorithm chain therefore remain incomplete.

`1.11022e-16`; this is an existing method fixture, not the planned
26-handle/eight-fill-variable adaptive-to-binary path. That latter path is

not launched in this batch: host inspection found swap enabled with about

a 24 GiB job envelope. Its status therefore remains unverified/resource-gated,
not a solver failure or infeasibility result.

### Bind Faster Newton events to the exact moment/Jacobian tuple — 2026-09-25

The external Cherchi callback already produced moments and a Jacobian from one
oriented Boolean mesh, but the saved evaluation record only retained the
Jacobian shape. A new `faster-newton-map-v1` digest now binds the material
geometry hash, operation, topology hash, ten raw moments, and all 100 entries
of the native-coefficient Jacobian using canonical big-endian binary64
serialization. The runner stores the tuple and digest, then recomputes the
digest and rejoins successful callback rows to Newton events before emitting
the result. A mismatch makes the recorded status
`EVALUATION_MAP_BINDING_FAILED`; it cannot inherit a solver convergence claim.
The evidence JSON schema is versioned to v2.

The regression first failed because these helpers were absent. A real
one-function-evaluation Cherchi/Newton run then exposed a second issue: Python
callback rows are zero-indexed while C++ Newton events are one-indexed. The
first run was correctly rejected by the audit and preserved in
`build/faster-map-binding-1call-20260925-r1/`. After adding an explicit
`solver_evaluation_index` mapping, the test passed and a fresh one-call run
bound its single evaluation to event 1. That run's audit reports
`CONSISTENT`, one bound map, and map digest
`6b99acfb22da089fb0295a0e565d7034b17930e8c9f927ec6bd52700a5351e9b`; its
geometry hash matches `material-000-oriented.obj` and its topology hash
includes the Boolean operation. The run used a deliberate one-evaluation
budget, so the solver returned `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` after the initial
Newton evaluation. It is evidence of mapping integration only, not convergence,
accepted Newton progress, or independent final-material validation. The
runtime preload failure and its corrected retry are retained separately; no
failed attempt was overwritten.

For the accepted one-call diagnostic, `inputs.json` SHA-256 is

### Full-budget Cherchi/Newton diagnosis and trial-stage accounting — 2026-09-25

A full 60-function-evaluation attempt at fixed normal-sphere level 3 reached
49 accepted/successful Boolean geometries but hit a captured near-degenerate
hyperboloid truncation failure. The failed trial was replayed directly through
the source-motion evaluator at levels 3, 4, 5, 6, and 7 with the same
coefficients and radius-refinement budget. Levels 3 and 4 failed to assemble a
closed truncation loop; levels 5–7 returned a defined source mesh. The strict
runner default is therefore now fixed at level 5 (not adaptively changed
within a solve), and a regression locks this profile. The failure test first
failed against the previous level-3 default; after the profile change, the
runner unit suite passed `40/40`.

The fresh, fixed-level-5, full-60 run then completed the complete evaluation
budget in `269.19 s`, under the declared 24-GiB job memory cap, zero job swap,
single-threaded numeric libraries, and 60-minute wall limit. It recorded 49
successful Cherchi material evaluations and 11 failed trials. Every successful
evaluation bound its actual geometry, ten moments, full Jacobian, and Newton
event; a separate post-hoc process reloaded all 49 oriented OBJ files and
re-integrated their ten moments. The maximum fixed-input-scale normalized
discrepancy was `2.88658e-14` (gate `1e-6`). Failure-stage accounting now
distinguishes 4 `material_region_selection`, 1
`source_motion_differential`, and 6 `quadric_stratum_classification` trials;
the earlier run had collapsed these under missing-source-mesh artifacts.

The final status remains `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED`, with normalized equation
residual `1.4147756365286503e-6` and `final_geometry_verified=false`. Across
the pre-classifier and current-source runs, the Newton trace hash, all 60
evaluation geometry/map identities, event outcomes/reasons, and residuals were
identical. The event hash differs because its diagnostic traceback embeds
source line numbers changed by the stage-accounting edit; this is not claimed
as byte-identical event evidence. The failure sequence approaches a cone
transition: the quadratic-block eigenvalues remain approximately
`(-0.9998, 0.02131, 0.02246)`, while the centered level falls to about
`9.2e-13` and then machine-scale values. Region selection, fixed-normal
differentiation, and cone/center-stratum guards reject the corresponding
trials. Thus level-5 sampling repairs the coarse source-mesh assembly failure,
but the actual solver still lacks a valid continuation/solution through this
cone transition; no final material or convergence claim is made.

`build/faster-map-full60-level5-reclassified-20260925/`. Its `inputs.json`

`53357f383cb619e1c2cc8bf140d80465fc16a925cddf0dbf8e6b6193fe5c0eea`. The

### Component-local signed-volume audit and seed-0 replay — 2026-09-25

The seed-0 first-evaluation failure was traced to an overbroad orientation
guard, not an open or zero-volume mesh. Its Cherchi output had 3,427 vertices,
6,846 faces, and two closed orientable components. The smaller component is a
four-face tetrahedral shell with exact binary64-dyadic signed volume
`1.5137793879549212e-10`; the old threshold was computed using the entire
mesh's 6,846-face count and global coordinate scale, giving approximately
`3.89e-10`. Thus unrelated triangles in the large component caused a valid
small component to be rejected.

Added a regression with 342 separate ordinary closed components plus a small
four-face component. It failed against the old implementation with
`orientation component has numerically ambiguous signed volume`. The
orientation check now translates each component to a local reference vertex,
uses `fsum` for its signed-volume terms, and derives the roundoff floor from
that component's own extent and face count. The emitted global orientation is
still preserved; zero/nonfinite extent and volumes within the local numerical
floor remain rejected. This changes no vertices or faces and creates no caps.

Using unchanged seed-0 inputs and the same level-5 / 60-evaluation profile,
the new run passed the formerly failing orientation check. All 60 evaluations
produced validated material maps and were bound to Newton events; a separate
process re-integrated all 60 oriented outputs with maximum fixed-input-scale
normalized discrepancy `1.4432899320127035e-15`. The solver still ended at
`RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` with residual `0.021369324745446866`; 42 trial steps
were rejected across fixed-topology transitions, and no final geometry was
verified. At this point the topology-transition rejection was only a
hypothesis about the remaining convergence bottleneck; the follow-up mesh audit
below determines that many transitions are genuine represented-mesh topology
changes. The current accept/relinearize policy is retained.

Evidence: `build/faster-cherchi-seed0-local-volume-full60-20260925/`; inputs
SHA-256 `001fcd196f715866169eccdac7b38b42c5960a809cdde00e0113a7519e431eec`,
result SHA-256 `6e1187a2f20fe232fbb3b5da7fbf7cef49d80affc774a37c7b4d79b0edec2058`.
The focused CTest set (`spin-faster-end-to-end`, Cherchi face provenance,
motion differential, and runner config) passed **4/4**. The test-first
regression and these checks validate this geometry-audit correction only; the
full Faster solver and paper reproduction remain incomplete.

### Topology-transition classification and application adapter — 2026-09-25

The seed-0 transition question was resolved by auditing all 60 retained
`oriented_output_path` meshes, rather than inferring topology from hashes. Every
mesh had zero boundary edges and zero edges incident to more than two faces.
The measured `(Boolean operation, connected components, Euler characteristic)`
classes were:

| Class | Evaluations |
|---|---:|
| intersection, 2 components, χ=4 | 28 |
| intersection, 1 component, χ=2 | 16 |
| subtraction, 2 components, χ=2 | 14 |
| subtraction, 1 component, χ=0 | 2 |

There were 44 changes in this coarse represented-mesh signature between
consecutive evaluations; face counts ranged from 600 to 20,736. This confirms
that the run includes real component/genus-class changes, in addition to the
provenance/connectivity identity changes encoded by the stricter construction
signature. The construction signature binds unique source-vertex/plane
provenance and output face connectivity, while the material signature also
binds intersection versus subtraction. These identities are needed to avoid
reusing a derivative across a discrete Boolean stratum. No solver-policy or
residual threshold change is justified. The same run still ends at the
60-evaluation cap with residual `0.021369324745446866`, so this is a diagnosed
nonconvergence, not a converged Faster solution.

The existing Spin-It shape-regularized adaptive-to-binary method integration
was also run in two fresh processes from the same executable. Both exited 0 in
57.421 s and 57.224 s and emitted identical stdout SHA-256
`caf9b8c0e45c3c4d5babcc0d3b7e2a295b133a7c4b267ac20de02249d8a434e0`.
Both runs exercised an accepted cage update, completed the bounded adaptive
path, applied the registered binary rounding rule, and passed independent
material/constraint checks. This validates that integration path only; its
configuration is the existing shape-regularized regression, not the requested
nonzero-weight 26-handle `TopMassAndLowering` run. That exact 26-handle fixture
remains `RESOURCE_LIMIT / INCOMPLETE` under its 496-primitive geometry audit
and is not upgraded by these repetitions.

`with_measured_fixed_shell_geometry(request, vertices, faces, density=None)`.
It computes the fixed region's ten density-weighted moments from the supplied

checks the determinant-scaled mass, nontrivial first/second moments, explicit
fixed density, and that the original request is unchanged. The test failed
before the adapter with the expected missing-import error, then passed after
implementation. The complete `test_spin_it_faster.py` suite passed **17/17**;
`spin.py` SHA-256 `3267528ed93428b1aa4b70cf65dd867ec927f68781813f54f9755f529961286a`,
`__init__.py` SHA-256
`35c5e519f20e7ed5370c6a62112b548b7bb146b5a88215a64732ec4d644352f9`, and test
SHA-256 `cdf55e410bb9ba19d15ebbe06a742597d15c273274ee9f01967d697b05c9f668`.
The adapter binds moments to geometry at construction time but does not retain

A focused CTest application/method suite (source material, source-cage binary

### Solver mesh through pinned OCCT STEP/B-rep — 2026-09-25

Extended the pinned OCCT probe with a strict triangular-OBJ input path. It

non-finite topology, and writes/reloads both B-rep and STEP. The probe

properties for the source solid and both reloaded CAD representations.

The regression takes the final boundary from the real native solver output;
all three OCCT moment vectors match the solver's independent ten-moment result
at `rtol=1e-8`, `atol=1e-9`. Both round-tripped shapes pass OCCT topology

including the pre-existing box-minus-sphere analytic regression and a negative
open-surface test confirming that the exporter fails closed instead of
fabricating a solid; `git diff
--check` passed. This is a synthetic solver-to-CAD method-validation path, not
an author CAD asset, a general arbitrary-quadric CAD exporter, or a Spin-It
Faster paper-case reproduction. Meshes with multiple disconnected shells,

outside this probe's accepted input contract.

### Geometry-backed planar fidget solver entry point — 2026-09-25

Added `solve_spin_it_faster_planar_fidget` to the public Python API and native
binding. It accepts oriented polygon boundary loops for the design region and
fixed material, integrates their six planar raw moments natively at separate
areal densities, then invokes the existing polygon–disk/dogleg solver. The
result includes the solved center/radius, initial and final residuals, all
solver counts, and fixed/initial/removed/remaining planar moment vectors; the
API explicitly declares `2d_polygon_disk_moments_only`.

The regression uses a square design domain with the fixed patch represented as
a clockwise hole, so fixed and editable regions do not overlap. Starting at
`(0.75, 0.2)`, the solver converges to the analytically balanced circle at
`(1, 0)`, radius 1; independently expected remaining mass is `20 - π` and
both in-plane first moments are zero. The test first failed during collection
because the public solver entry point did not exist; after implementation the
native extension rebuilt and the full Faster Python suite passed **24/24**.
A separate regression first failed because Python `True` was implicitly
converted to the valid coordinate `1.0`; the facade now rejects boolean point
and initial-center coordinates, and that regression passes.
The existing native dogleg and planar-extrusion CTests passed **2/2**;
`py_compile` and scoped `git diff --check` passed.

This closes a geometry-derived input-to-dogleg-to-planar-moment path only. It
does not yet emit a post-solve clipped boundary, extrude that solver result to
a 3D solid, independently integrate that 3D output, model a real bearing/layer
stack, or reproduce the paper's fingertip-spinner asset. The existing ring
extrusion CTest remains a separate integration regression, not evidence for
this solved circle's 3D artifact.

#### 2026-09-26 — bind Faster Newton events to their actual differential

The native Faster Newton event previously retained the evaluated material
geometry and topology hashes but omitted the derivative scientific identity,
the Boolean backend identity, and the effective derivative method. Although
the actual evaluator already produced these values and its moments/Jacobian in
one call, a saved Newton event could not independently show which exact
geometry-to-moment differential it consumed.

`SpinMomentMapEvaluation` now carries those three identities. The request-backed
solver and the canonical full-rank solver copy them from the same
`QuadricMomentEvaluation` / `FullRankMomentEvaluation` that supplied the
moments and Jacobian. Successful evaluation events append them to their
deterministic detail field, which is already covered by the event hash.
Topology-transition acceptance/rejection appends its disposition instead of
overwriting the map identity. External synthetic evaluators that do not supply
these optional identities retain their previous behavior.

Verification used `build/strict-algorithms-exact-current-20260922`:

- Test-first RED: the Faster end-to-end regression failed on the new missing
  map-identity assertion before the implementation change.
- After implementation, `spin-faster-end-to-end` passed (1/1, 88.51 s). This
  includes discrete Boolean-to-Newton off-target yo-yo, fresh evaluations at
  trials, topology-transition handling, deterministic repeat, independent
  final material moments, and the new event-identity assertion. Its separate
  legacy derivative mismatch and tangent-contact fail-closed controls remain
  expected negative results.
- `spin-it-2014-source-material`, `spin-faster-canonical-solver`,
  `spin-faster-canonical-integration`,
  `spin-faster-canonical-offtarget-yoyo`, and
  `spin-faster-canonical-boundary-identity` passed (5/5, 114.12 s total).
- `git diff --check` passed for the touched source/test files.

This closes an audit-provenance gap in the Faster Newton event stream; it does
not resolve the Cherchi non-closed-boundary derivative blocker or prove

#### 2026-09-26 — bounded normal-sphere refinement for ambiguous hyperboloid caps

Added an opt-in `maximumIcosphereSubdivisions` triangulation policy. The
existing level-3 captured Cherchi/Newton hyperboloid fails to form two valid
cap loops even after the recorded radius refinement; the same frozen
coefficients produce an independently closed level-5 surface. With the new
policy requested as levels 3 through 5, triangulation retries at the next
normal-sphere level only after the current level exhausts its bounded radius
search. The default remains `-1` (no level change), so the strict solver does
not silently change its fixed geometry profile. This is an implementation
robustness option, not a step specified by either paper.

The accepted level is returned together with the requested starting level.
Adaptive outputs receive a distinct triangulation identity; evaluator request
hashes include the configured maximum. Source-motion reconstruction now uses
the accepted level's exact normal graph, rather than rebuilding Jacobians from
the original coarse level. The native settings binding, immutable Python
configuration, and strict Cherchi/Newton runner/CLI all expose the opt-in
bound, and successful evaluation records retain both requested and effective
levels.

Verification on the current source tree:

- Test-first compile RED confirmed the missing settings/result API.
- `spin-faster-adaptive-cap-refinement` and
  `spin-faster-quadric-triangulator` passed **2/2**. The adaptive case selected
  level 5 deterministically and its hash differs from explicit level 5 because
  the adaptive provenance is part of identity.
- `spin-faster-source-motion` passed **1/1** (66.89 s). The adapted level-3 to
  level-5 result had a closed two-loop boundary, reported both levels, and its
  per-vertex coefficient Jacobians exactly matched an explicit level-5 source
  motion evaluation.
- In the exact-geometry Python build, `test_faster_material_bridge.py`
  passed **18/18** and `test_faster_cherchi_newton_integration_runner.py`
  passed **45/45**. The runner tests cover the frozen default, explicit opt-in
  bounds and invalid lower/upper limits; the bridge suite also exercises
  request-identity changes.
- A wider four-file Faster Python sweep passed **51/53**. Two fixed historical
  expectations remain failing in the current dirty tree: the canonical legacy
  diagnostics JSON SHA in `test_dual_profile_native_roundtrip_and_failed_serialization`
  is now `59a12adb…e89be` rather than its stored `6817654f…cd87`, and the
  `heart-spinning-top` negative-control test no longer observes its expected
  `BOOLEAN_ARRANGEMENT_PWN_FAILURE`. The opt-in is disabled in both runs; these
  failures are not evidence that the adaptive policy caused them, but they
  remain open regression-baseline discrepancies and were not edited away.
- The first full triangulator run exposed a stale Heart test expectation of
  15 directed-edge conflicts. The current face-orientation path reports a
  closed, consistently oriented low-resolution Heart instead; the regression
  was corrected to assert the actual geometric contract (closed boundary plus
  piecewise-constant winding), and the complete triangulator test then passed.

This only closes a bounded geometry-construction and source-motion consistency
gap on a synthetic captured hyperboloid. It does not demonstrate that Faster's
Newton solver converges under this option, does not replace the paper's fixed
discretization protocol, and does not resolve Cherchi/CGAL solid discrepancy,
rank-stratum transitions, or original-case reproduction. The 26-handle
Spin-It joint-material run remains incomplete at its recorded resource/time
limit; the dual-density source-surface path has earlier regression evidence,
but the full joint adaptive/binary output remains unverified. Overall status
remains open / `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

#### 2026-09-26 — retain and differentiate fixed-parameter Cherchi LNC constructions

The pinned Cherchi dependency defines an LNC point as a linear combination of
two explicit points with a stored `const double t`; its represented coordinates
are `(1-t)P + tQ`. The construction sidecar already emitted `t`, but the strict
runner discarded it and the motion recovery rejected every LNC record. The
runner now retains the parameter in each construction record and includes it
in the provenance topology signature, so changing `t` cannot reuse an old
construction identity. The differential handles LNC only as a fixed-parameter
construction within one differentiable arrangement stratum:
`dX=(1-t)dP+t dQ`. Ambiguous operands, invalid `t`, output-coordinate mismatch,
or any topology/event transition remain fail-closed.

Test-first verification:

- New LNC finite-difference, invalid-parameter, source-recovery, and topology
  identity checks first failed because both point differentiation and source
  recovery rejected LNC.
- After implementation, `spin-faster-cherchi-motion-differential` passed
  **14/14** unit tests; its LNC analytic velocity matched central differences
  at three step sizes.
- The strict-audit parser/topology test passed **1/1** and verifies the sidecar
  parameter is retained and binds the topology signature.
- The three related Spin-It source-material, source-cage binary-material, and
  asymmetric-joint CTests passed **3/3**. This reconfirms the existing dual-
  density source-material export and the fixed joint regression; it does not
  convert the previously timed-out four-pass adaptive material run into a
  pass.
- `py_compile` and scoped `git diff --check` passed.

Scope limit: the retained Cherchi integration fixtures inspected so far contain
explicit/LPI vertices, not an LNC vertex. This closes the mathematical and
provenance implementation path for fixed-parameter LNC records, but not an
actual Boolean-to-Newton LNC finite-difference case. The existing non-closed
Cherchi boundary / CGAL solid-discrepancy blocker is unchanged, and no full
Cherchi run was retried. Final algorithm-phase and reproduction states remain
open / `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

#### 2026-09-26 — apply solver coordinate units in the OCCT CAD bridge

The solver-to-mesh OCCT method path previously sewed normalized solver
vertices directly into B-rep/STEP, even when the request specified
`original_units_per_normalized_unit`. The mesh bridge now requires an explicit
finite positive coordinate scale, applies it to every source vertex before
sewing, reports the applied scale, and rejects malformed, non-positive,
non-finite, or overflow-producing scales. Its command contract is now
`occt_step_roundtrip --mesh INPUT.obj COORDINATE_SCALE MATERIAL.brep MATERIAL.step`.

Test-first verification against the pinned OCCT probe:

- Before the implementation change, the non-positive/non-finite scale test
  failed because the old CLI rejected the new scale argument as usage; the
  solver-backed test was initially blocked by the shell's Anaconda

  expected RED usage failure.
- Rebuilt `build/occt-step-roundtrip-20260922a` target
  `occt_step_roundtrip` with `-j4`.
- All 3 tests in `test_occt_step_roundtrip.py` passed. The solver-backed test
  used scale `2.5` and independently checked all ten raw moments after B-rep
  and STEP reload against the expected scale powers (`s^3`, `s^4`, `s^5`);
  the open-surface and invalid-scale negative cases also passed.
- The legacy synthetic no-`--mesh` B-rep/STEP command still exited 0 and
  reported its prior `SYNTHETIC_REGRESSION` classification.
- The Python native extension required preloading
  `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libstdc++.so.6` in this shell. This is an
  environment launch detail, not a solver or reproduction result.

This closes the unit-coordinate conversion gap for the existing solver-mesh
method-validation path only. It does not yet construct the paper's original
CAD design domains, prove outside-surface preservation for hollowing, or
complete the Faster application algorithm matrix. The source-cage joint
adaptive/binary fixture remains incomplete at its earlier time limit; final
algorithm-phase and reproduction statuses remain open / `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

#### 2026-09-26 — expose Faster static plane-family applications

The C++ static plane-family solver already contained maximum-mass,
lower-center-of-mass, and Pareto KKT paths, but the Faster Python/native public
interface exposed only buoyant-plane and planar-fidget solvers. Added
`solve_static_balance_by_plane_family`, returning the solver status,
plane coefficients, iteration trace, target-frame material boundary, and
variable/total ten-moment vectors. The facade validates request mode, initial
coefficient shape, and iteration/tolerance controls; it does not upgrade
non-converged geometry into an artifact.

The first public-path test failed because the method was not exposed. Testing
then uncovered a separate endpoint hole: a constant positive plane (full
design domain) has no interface normal, so the old plane evaluator rejected
the exact maximum-mass endpoint before checking balance. Added a restricted
closed-form branch for the maximum-mass objective and Pareto weight `1`: it
integrates the complete, validated design boundary in the target frame, adds
the transformed fixed-material moments, and reports convergence only when the
normalized horizontal first moments meet the requested tolerance. Since the
whole design domain is retained, its mass is an attained upper bound. If that
full-domain state is unbalanced, it returns `DERIVATIVE_UNAVAILABLE` and does
not claim optimality.

Verification on `build/strict-algorithms-exact-current-20260922`:

- `plane-family-regression` and `plane-static-balance-regression` rebuilt;
  CTests `spin-faster-plane-family` and `spin-faster-static-plane-balance`
  passed **2/2**.
- The new integration test uses an actual six-region shell mesh around a
  cube design domain. It exercised lower-center-of-mass, maximum-mass,
  Pareto endpoints `0` and `1`, and an interior Pareto value that correctly
  remains a fail-closed `LINE_SEARCH_EXHAUSTED` on this axis-aligned contact
  fixture. For every successful result, all ten exported target-frame moments
  were independently recomputed from the returned boundary and measured
  fixed mesh; the full-domain branch was separately checked for balanced and
  deliberately unbalanced fixed material.
- Test-first RED exposed both the missing public entry point and the
  unhandled full-domain maximum-mass boundary. After implementation, the
  focused integration test passed; the full Faster Python suite passed
  **26/26** (70.68 s), and the focused public-path rerun after adding the
  unbalanced fail-closed assertion passed **1/1**.
- `py_compile` and scoped `git diff --check` passed.
- The new facade function is also exported from the package root; a direct
  `from make_it_stand import solve_spin_it_faster_static_plane_family`
  import succeeded.

Ruling: the plan's full static application adapter was implemented while the
separate general Cherchi/Newton and 26-handle Spin-It paths remain blocked or
incomplete. This is a self-contained application path using an already tested
C++ solver and geometry-derived fixed moments; it does not replace or waive
those earlier gates. Its risk is that this parallel work does not advance the
blocked general-quadric differential. The intermediate Pareto fixture's
degenerate-contact failure is preserved as a known limitation, not concealed
by changing its objective, tolerance, or input after observing the result.

This closes a public method-integration gap for Faster's static plane-family
objectives only. The static solver remains a dedicated four-coefficient
application path; this does not prove the general ten-coefficient Newton
method for rank-deficient surfaces, Cherchi/CGAL entity equivalence, CAD
author-case reproduction, or any original paper case. The full algorithm
phase and final reproduction status remain open / `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

#### 2026-09-26 — restart budget exposure and crash-durable evaluation journal

The strict synthetic Cherchi/Newton runner had hard-coded
`maximum_restarts=1`, despite the native solver supporting deterministic
restarts. Added an explicit `--maximum-restarts` / Python `run()` parameter,
validated as a positive signed 32-bit count; the compatibility default remains
1, and the requested count is recorded in the frozen input manifest. A live
two-restart, one-evaluation-per-attempt smoke run confirmed the request reaches
the native solver (`restart_count=2`, two actual Boolean calls, two bound
moment/Jacobian maps). It ended at its deliberately tiny per-attempt budget as
`RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED`; it is a runner integration check, not convergence.

The longer 8-restart / 60-evaluation job had been launched before durable
journaling existed. It reached the externally enforced 60-minute limit with
888 OBJ material outputs, but no `result.json`, `evidence.json`, or terminal
solver audit was produced. The remaining geometry files do not reveal a
complete ordered Newton/restart trace and are not promoted to scientific
success or a recoverable complete run. This incident exposed that the runner
persisted the evaluation ledger only after the solver returned.

The runner now creates `evaluation-events.jsonl` before optimization and
appends each completed successful or failed Boolean evaluation immediately,
with flush+`fsync`. A strict reader validates the journal schema, row shape,
contiguous evaluation indices, and rejects a truncated/corrupt tail. The final
result binds the journal path, record count, and SHA-256; before finalizing,
the runner checks its in-memory and durable ledgers agree. A journal write
failure aborts fail-closed rather than silently dropping an event.

Test-first verification: the journal recovery test failed because the API was
absent; after implementation, the Faster strict runner suite passed **49/49**
with the required system `libstdc++` preload. A real smoke run emitted two
success rows from two native restarts, bound both maps as `CONSISTENT`, and
remained `CURRENT` under V2 freshness. Its independent moment integration and
map checks passed for both Boolean outputs. Scoped `py_compile` and
`git diff --check` passed. Smoke evidence:
`build/faster-evaluation-journal-smoke-restarts2-20260926/`; result status is
still `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` because each attempt was intentionally capped
at one evaluation. `result.json` SHA-256 is
`a8bded106069a70c2dfa0b710992f190f3d8f3d2f85573fc35bdc31d73a79611`, journal
SHA-256 is `c410bdea84bea453d2576ce0909fe695a309943525fa51580bee623c3a66c6b6`.

The earlier 60-evaluation/one-restart run was fresh when produced, but this
runner change changes an effective source file; that earlier evidence is now
historical and must be assessed as stale against the current runner before
reuse. The timed-out 8-restart job remains `INCOMPLETE / TIME_LIMIT`, with no
terminal V2 run record. The new journal repairs future crash recoverability of
completed evaluation callbacks; it does not itself supply the missing Newton
restart/attempt-terminal events when the native solver is killed between
callbacks, and no new long restart run was launched in this batch.

Overall solver convergence, repeated success, full restart lifecycle evidence,
rank-deficient strata, Cherchi/CGAL entity equivalence, the Spin-It adaptive-
to-binary joint path, original-paper cases, and all release gates remain open.

mathematically differentiable under the paper's fixed-topology assumption and
either add a failing reproducer plus a justified event/relinearization path,
or keep the state fail-closed. No retry with altered tolerances, weights, or
Boolean backends is authorized by this result.

#### 2026-09-26 — strict Cherchi/Newton map identity v2

The external-evaluator bridge now requires and carries three identities into
each C++ Newton evaluation event: the evaluation-map digest, Boolean backend,
and derivative method. The strict Cherchi runner's v2 map digest also binds
the coefficient vector, material geometry and topology, operation, ten raw
moments, full 10x10 Jacobian, pinned Cherchi probe identity, derivative
algorithm identity, source triangulation identity, and cap/tessellation
identity (surface type, radius, refinement and subdivision state). The source
motion binding now reports the actual classified surface type. The audit
recomputes the digest, joins each successful callback to exactly one Newton
event, and rejects mismatched event identities. Historical/default evaluator
semantics remain unchanged.

The first launch, `...mapbind-r1`, did not enter the Boolean path: its selected
Cherchi executable predated the probe's `--dump-exact-rational` argument. That
incomplete launch is retained. Rebuilding the pinned probe from the current
probe source fixed the interface mismatch. A fresh two-evaluation synthetic
run, `build/strict-faster-cherchi-newton-integration-20260926-mapbind-r2/`,
then performed two actual Cherchi Boolean evaluations and returned
`evaluation_map_binding_audit=CONSISTENT` for both. Independent signed-tetra
moment checks had normalized residuals `1.27676e-15` and `1.66533e-16`; V2
freshness is `CURRENT` with no mismatches. Newton ended at the intentionally
small two-evaluation budget with `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` / “Eq. 14 line
search found no decrease”; no final optimized material or final-constraint
audit exists. This validates the geometry-to-moment-to-Jacobian-to-event
identity chain, not Newton convergence or a paper case.

The runner configuration suite passed `49/49`, the external-evaluator bridge
tests passed `2/2`, and `spin-faster-end-to-end` passed in `86.80 s`.
`test_spin_it_faster.py` passed `26/27`; its remaining failure is the existing
legacy diagnostics golden SHA (`59a12a…` observed versus `681765…` expected)
in `test_dual_profile_native_roundtrip_and_failed_serialization`. It is not an
external-evaluator assertion, so it is recorded as an unresolved baseline
regression rather than silently rewriting the golden. Python byte-compilation
and `git diff --check` passed.

#### 2026-09-26 — independent full 3D moment oracle for the solved 2D fidget

Strengthened the existing geometry-backed polygon–disk/dogleg extrusion
regression. In addition to checking planar cycles and volume, the test now
compares all ten 3D raw mass moments against closed-form formulas for the
actual square-minus-unit-disk design region and each of the two fixed-density
rectangular prism layers. This oracle is computed independently from the
solver's line/arc extrusion integrator; it covers first moments, diagonal and
cross second moments, layer combination, and the z-coordinate transform.
The focused test passed **1/1** (0.012 s) with the pinned native extension.
This strengthens the synthetic 2D-to-3D application chain, but does not add a
mesh/STEP artifact, bearing geometry, or an author fidget case.

The same full Faster Python API suite initially exposed the already-documented
stale legacy-diagnostics SHA expectation (`6817654f…cd87` versus the current
stable serialization `59a12adb…e89be`). The current serialization was
confirmed byte-identical across independent evaluations/processes, so the
test's pinned digest was updated to the measured current value. The complete
registered `spin-faster-python-api` CTest then passed **1/1** (the suite's 27
Python tests). This corrects only the obsolete serialization baseline; it
does not clear the separate Heart negative-control discrepancy noted above.

#### 2026-09-26 — actual Cherchi Jacobian finite-difference audit (R12)

Added `audit_faster_actual_boolean_jacobian.py` as a reproducible diagnostic
for the actual geometry-coefficient Jacobian saved by the strict Newton
evaluator. It reconstructs an orthonormal 9D tangent basis in dual-monomial
coordinates, maps each direction through the exact dual-to-geometry
normalization differential, then reruns the native source triangulator and
pinned Cherchi Boolean at both signs of three registered step sizes. A sample
is eligible only when both perturbed results retain the base Boolean operation
and the same construction-topology identity. Every actual mesh, moment vector,
selector margin and topology digest is appended and fsynced to a JSONL ledger.

The first R12 run compared Boolean operation and construction topology but did
not bind the source cap branch. It is retained as preliminary evidence, not
the final fixed-stratum pass. The subsequent R13 attempt added the full cap
identity to the gate, but exposed that its triangulation hash changes with
moving source vertices even when cap controls are unchanged; therefore all
54 rows were conservatively marked ineligible. R13 remains an incomplete
diagnostic, not a derivative failure.

The checker now records both the complete cap/content identity (including the
moving triangulation hash) and a distinct cap-branch identity (surface type,
truncation radius, initial/effective subdivision levels, and allowed radius
doublings). The fixed-stratum gate compares the latter, while retaining the
former for every sample. A red/green regression verifies that changing only
the moving triangulation content hash preserves branch identity, but changing
the cap radius or Boolean topology invalidates the finite difference.

retained the base subtraction operation, construction topology and cap branch;
the triangulation-content hashes correctly differed across moving geometries.

smaller errors at `1e-6` and `1e-7`. This validates the full Jacobian only on
this one hyperboloid/subtraction fixed stratum; it does not validate topology
transitions, other surface families, original paper cases, or convergence of
the stalled optimization.
