#### 2026-09-26 — dual-material rejection regressions

Extended the existing nonempty source-surface dual-density integration test
with two fail-closed checks against its actual exported shell/interior meshes.
The independent audit now has regression evidence that substituting an
incorrect shell density is rejected by the ten-moment comparison, and that
omitting the interior material cannot be treated as a zero-density phase or
accepted as a complete dual-material result. No solver configuration or
geometry was changed. The registered `source-spin-material-python` CTest
passed **1/1** in 37.48 seconds, including the unequal-density success path,
equal-density legacy path, and both new negative assertions. This closes the
missing-density-error-test item only; it does not close adaptive joint-to-
binary optimization or Sumone's empty-inner result.

The follow-up review then required two additional integrity checks: recompute
the native quadric coefficient digest, and reject incomplete but self-hashed
cap payloads. `QuadricCoefficients10::hashNormalized` now computes the same
versioned native digest without renormalizing solver coefficients; the strict
runner records it and requires exact equality with the Newton event's hash and
coefficient vector. The cap payload is checked for its full fixed schema,
surface token, positive finite hexadecimal radius, bounded subdivision and
radius-refinement values, and triangulation SHA before its digest is trusted.
The added tests first failed on arbitrary hashes and missing cap fields; they
pass after the changes. One intermediate attempt (`r4`) exposed that native
surface tokens are uppercase; this was corrected and retained as failed
diagnostic evidence.

It still stops at the preregistered two-function-evaluation cap with

terminal rejection is an actual operation/Boolean-stratum transition, not a
hash changing merely because coefficients changed. The trace does not reach
another same-stratum trial after evaluation 60, so it cannot establish whether
backtracking would eventually find a decreasing step.

the cap was reached immediately before the first same-operation trial could
be evaluated. This does not show that the trial would decrease the residual;

### Exact next-step replay (R9; diagnostic only)

Added an explicit `exact_line_search_replay` mode to the strict runner. It
requires ten finite, unit-norm coefficients, rejects that option for other
initialization modes, and labels the run `DIAGNOSTIC_REPLAY`; it does not alter
the original 60-evaluation runs. Configuration tests cover the mode, unit norm,
CLI parsing, and fail-closed validation before output-directory creation.

The next same-operation point was reconstructed from accepted event 53 and
trial event 54 using the recorded dual-monomial spherical geodesic. The
reconstruction reproduces event 60's `0.0078125` coefficients with maximum
absolute difference `2.78e-17`, then evaluates the next halved step
`0.00390625` in a new one-evaluation run. The new actual Cherchi result is the
subtraction branch, map-binding audit `CONSISTENT`, independent moment audit
`PASSED` with normalized infinity residual `1.11e-15`, and freshness `CURRENT`.
At that point the independently recomputed normalized equation residual is
`0.3093748235561487`, versus `0.30868070927911745` at the accepted base: an
increase of `6.9411427703e-4` (`0.2249%`). Thus this same-operation trial would
also be rejected by the current residual-decrease rule. It is not evidence of
infeasibility, convergence, or a final verified design. The one-evaluation
diagnostic reports `RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED` by design and is not counted as a
solver attempt in the original run.

### R11/R12: full-budget Cherchi restart accounting

R11 extended the same frozen, answer-independent synthetic regression to two
actual restarts at the paper's full 60-evaluation budget per attempt. It
completed in 418.82 s under the 24 GiB, zero-swap, 60-minute job limits. Both
attempts terminated at 60 evaluations with
`fixed-topology derivative guard rejected Boolean topology transition`;
the final recorded normalized equation residual was `1.6637632104e-2`.
There was no final verified geometry. All 120 solver/evaluator map bindings
were `CONSISTENT`, independent moments passed (maximum normalized residual
`1.72e-15`), and evidence freshness was `CURRENT`.

R12 then used the same frozen input, binary, backend, and seed for five full
restarts (the registered restart protocol), completing in 1751.12 s under the
same resource limits. The run is `SYNTHETIC_REGRESSION`, ends at
`RESOURCE_LIMIT_REACHED`, and has no final geometry. All five attempts used
exactly 60 evaluations and terminated with `maximum_evaluations_reached`.
The last recorded normalized equation residual was `1.2548958343e-2`; this is
not a convergence or infeasibility certificate. All 300 evaluator-to-Newton
map bindings were `CONSISTENT`, the maximum independently recomputed
ten-moment residual was `1.07e-14`, and evidence freshness was `CURRENT` with
no mismatches. The 300 evaluation outcomes comprise 5 initial accepts, 2
residual-decrease accepts, 64 topology-transition accepts after
relinearization, and 229 topology-transition rejections. The first 120 map
identities agree with R11's deterministic prefix. These results establish
actual Cherchi-to-Newton map binding and complete restart/failure accounting;
they do not establish solver convergence, a feasible final design, or general
nonconvergence.

A read-only partition of R12's 300 actual Boolean results narrows the next
diagnostic: all use icosphere subdivision level 5 and the same maximum of four
cap-radius doublings; 291 use one truncation radius and 9 use the doubled
radius. In contrast, the material output has 190 distinct vertex/face-count
pairs (34–10,444 vertices; 64–20,880 faces) and 272 distinct material and
construction topology hashes. This weighs against subdivision-level churn as
the main cause and is consistent with the moving quadric crossing many
Boolean topology strata. It does not yet distinguish true arrangement
changes from cap-radius effects or provenance attribution, so no solver fix is
inferred from it.

Joining solver events to their exact evaluator records shows 224 of 229
topology-transition rejections used the ordinary truncation radius; only 5
used the doubled radius. Of the 64 accepted-and-relinearized transitions, 60
used the ordinary radius and 4 the doubled radius. Thus cap-radius expansion
is present but cannot explain most rejected transitions in this batch.

### R13: exact-input Cherchi determinism replay

To test whether a repeated identical quadric can itself produce spurious
topology changes, the exact coefficients from R12's rejected transition at
solver evaluation 5 were replayed once in each of two independent processes.
Both used the strict runner, the pinned single-thread Cherchi probe, the same
native extension, level-5 triangulation, and an explicit one-evaluation
diagnostic budget. Both actual Boolean outputs have identical geometry,
material-topology, construction-topology, cap, and source-identity hashes;
the material OBJ, construction provenance TSV, and face provenance TSV bytes
also match exactly. Both map-binding audits are `CONSISTENT`, independent
ten-moment audits are `PASSED` with normalized infinity residual
`3.64e-17`, and evidence freshness is `CURRENT` with no mismatches. Each
diagnostic stops at its intentionally exhausted one-evaluation budget and
does not claim a final solver result.

This rules out nondeterminism for this exact repeated Boolean input, but does
not establish that all changes between different quadrics are true geometric
arrangement transitions rather than a provenance or cap effect. Together
with R12's 224/229 rejected transitions at the ordinary cap radius, it
supports auditing the transition identity and local derivative across
different quadrics; it does not justify weakening the fail-closed topology
guard.

### R14: independent topology-invariant audit of all R12 outputs

All 300 R12 Boolean OBJ outputs were independently loaded and audited. Every
one is watertight and winding-consistent. Comparing each line-search candidate
to the last accepted mesh within its restart, 172 of 229 rejected
`topology_transition` candidates changed connected-component/Euler
invariants, and 57 preserved those coarse invariants while changing the
strict construction-topology identity. Of the 64 accepted/relinearized
transitions, 14 changed those invariants and 50 did not. Among rejected
transitions, 160 changed both the selected Boolean operation and coarse
topology; 3 changed operation without changing those coarse invariants; 12
kept the operation but changed coarse topology; and 54 kept both operation
and coarse topology while changing the construction identity.

The direct mesh witnesses confirm that at least most rejected transitions are
real changes in the represented material topology, not hash-only noise. The
remaining same-invariant transitions still change the triangle/source-feature
construction complex used by the discrete derivative, so Euler/connected
component equality alone is not enough to reuse a Jacobian. This evidence
does not prove that every such event is mathematically nondifferentiable; it
does show that replacing the fail-closed guard with a coarse topology test
would be unjustified. No solver guard or threshold was changed.

### R23: coefficient-driven analytic sphere Boolean to OCCT STEP

Extended the pinned OCCT roundtrip probe with `--quadric-sphere`. It parses
the solver's ten-coefficient convention `[c,bx,by,bz,qxx,qyy,qzz,qyz,qxz,qxy]`,
requires a unit-norm coefficient vector and an isotropic positive-definite
quadratic block, completes the square to recover center/radius, constructs an
OCCT analytic sphere, and subtracts it from a sewn closed input domain. It
then validates the Boolean solid, writes/reloads B-rep and STEP, and returns
all ten raw moments. It rejects nonspherical coefficients rather than
triangulating or approximating them. The current option interprets the input
coefficients in the already-transformed coordinates of the CAD domain and
removes the sphere interior. At the time this probe was added, its caller had
to supply that CAD-frame conversion; R24 adds a separate target-frame pullback
for the sphere path. Ellipsoids, paraboloids, hyperboloids, and multiple
material solids remain unsupported.

The regression uses a translated radius-0.5 sphere inside `[-2,2]^3` and
compares all ten B-rep and STEP moments against an independent closed-form
box-minus-sphere oracle. A non-isotropic ellipsoid coefficient set is a
fail-closed negative case. The test was observed failing on the old CLI due to
unsupported arguments, then passed after implementation. The OCCT target
rebuilt successfully; the complete `test_occt_step_roundtrip.py` passed 6/6.
The solver-mesh path and synthetic box-minus-sphere path remain intact.

Final verification for this implementation turn: `test_faster_material_bridge.py`
passed 24/24 after the A/upright, B/tilted and B/reversed pose checks were
added; the API imports through `make_it_stand.verification`, all edited Python
files compile, and `git diff --check` passes. The earlier native 7/7 CTest
subset is unchanged by this Python-only addition and was not rerun here.

### R24: Faster target-frame quadric pullback to CAD coordinates

Added `quadric_coefficients_to_cad_frame`, which applies the request's
right-handed target axes/origin and `original_units_per_normalized_unit` to
the homogeneous 4x4 quadric matrix, then restores canonical ten-coefficient
ordering and unit norm. It rejects non-unit solver input coefficients,
reflected/non-orthonormal frames, non-finite values, and invalid coordinate
scale. The OCCT regression now starts with target-frame sphere coefficients,
uses a translated 90-degree target frame and 2.5 coordinate scale, transforms
the coefficients to CAD space, and checks the exported sphere center/radius
and all ten raw B-rep/STEP moments against an independent analytic oracle.

### R25: Solver ellipsoid quadrics through analytic OCCT Boolean and STEP

Extended the CAD probe from spheres to strictly positive-definite ellipsoid
quadrics, including off-diagonal terms. The adapter completes the square,
checks positive definiteness, maps an analytic unit sphere by an affine
transform, subtracts that OCCT B-rep from the sewn design domain, and
round-trips the resulting material through B-rep and STEP. It rejects
non-ellipsoid or non-positive-definite inputs rather than approximating them
with a mesh. `quadric_coefficients_to_cad_frame` is exercised with translated,
rotated target coordinates and a non-unit coordinate scale.

The first integration check exposed a roughly `4.4e-4` relative error from
the default OCCT volume-properties route on the transformed sphere surface.
This was traced to integration accuracy, not the quadric pullback: the
coefficient-derived center, affine map, and determinant volume match the
analytic ellipsoid. The probe now uses OCCT's adaptive volume integration
with `Eps=1e-10` and rejects failed or insufficient integration estimates.
The final regression compares all ten B-rep and STEP raw moments, plus the
emitted ellipsoid volume, against an independent closed-form rotated
ellipsoid-in-box oracle at a `3e-8` relative/absolute-scale test threshold.

### R26: Rotated elliptic-cylinder quadric to bounded OCCT CAD

Added a coefficient-driven elliptic-cylinder CAD path. It validates the
unit-normalized solver coefficient vector, recovers and canonicalizes the
rank-two null axis, diagonalizes the transverse quadratic form, rejects a
linear term along the cylinder axis, and derives the closest-axis point and
both ellipse radii. The unbounded cylinder is represented by an affine exact
OCCT cylinder carrier whose finite caps are placed outside the design-domain
AABB projection; the Boolean therefore clips the cylinder against the actual
closed domain rather than introducing cap material in the domain. The
resulting material is topology-checked, exported/reloaded as B-rep and STEP,
and independently checked against a closed-form ten-moment oracle for a
translated, rotated elliptic cylinder inside a co-oriented cuboid.

A negative regression verifies that a rank-two quadric with an axial linear
term (a paraboloid family member, not a cylinder) is rejected before writing
CAD output. Circular cylinders are included as the repeated-transverse-
eigenvalue case. This path does not claim general paraboloid or hyperboloid
CAD support, and the probe is not yet automatically wired to every Faster
solver result.

`test_faster_material_bridge.py` passed 33/33. The OCCT probe rebuilt, Python

### R27: Elliptic-paraboloid quadric to bounded OCCT CAD

Added a CAD path for elliptic paraboloids from normalized solver quadric

principal transverse scales, and builds the exact meridian parabola as a
quadratic Bezier profile revolved into a finite carrier. An affine transform
places that carrier in the solver frame. Its radial wall and top cap are
constructed outside the finite design-domain AABB projection, so Boolean
clipping represents the unbounded paraboloid sublevel set within the domain
without introducing carrier faces there. The resulting material is validated
and round-tripped through B-rep and STEP.

their mass moments against the solver's complete material moments. Density is
carried as an ordered sidecar associated with the solids; it is not encoded as
a native STEP material property. The adapter fails closed on mismatched density
metadata, missing solver convergence, invalid cycles, and pre-existing output
paths.

Verification on 2026-09-26: the pinned OCCT probe rebuilt, and the combined
OCCT, Faster, and evaluation-capture regressions passed 49/49 using the same
current Python extension built with exact-geometry checks and Faster enabled.
The layered case retains three solids (one editable extrusion and two fixed
layers); STEP-reloaded total ten-moment error is below `5e-8`, and the negative
wrong-density case is rejected. An initial run against a build with exact
geometry checks disabled was not counted: after rebuilding with the required
feature enabled and selecting that exact extension, the full targeted suite
passed.

This closes a synthetic method-level handoff, not arbitrary layered CAD
coverage or a paper fidget-spinner experiment. Curved fixed-layer boundaries,
general layer arrangements, intrinsic density/material attributes, author
inputs and parameters, Cherchi source provenance and backend equivalence,
remaining quadric families, author Floating Island inputs/protocol, original
paper matrices, and final physical/manufacturing gates remain open. Overall
status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

### R36: Exact hyperbolic-paraboloid CAD Boolean and STEP transport

Added a fail-closed OCCT path for rank-two saddle quadrics with one positive
and one negative transverse eigenvalue and a nonzero linear term along the
null axis. It constructs the saddle as an exact tensor-product quadratic
Bezier surface, sweeps a finite carrier beyond the design-domain bounds,
performs the quadric/design-domain Boolean, and validates the resulting B-rep
and STEP reload. Both axial-linear signs are covered; the sign reversal changes
the retained side and canonical axis. A zero axial-linear term is explicitly
rejected as a cylinder rather than mislabeled as a paraboloid.

The regression uses a translated, obliquely rotated saddle and compares all ten
raw moments for the material and its STEP/B-rep round trips against independent
closed-form integrals (relative/absolute tolerances `8e-8`); a repeated
independent OCCT process returns the same scientific JSON byte-for-byte. The
path now also refuses pre-existing/equal output paths before writing, preserving
prior evidence. Targeted saddle, rank-classification, and overwrite-protection
tests passed **3/3**; the complete pinned-OCCT Python round-trip suite passed
**19/19** using the current Faster Python extension.

This is a synthetic CAD-method result. It does not add a hyperbolic-paraboloid
Newton optimizer or prove general rank-deficient optimization, arbitrary CAD
design-domain equivalence, Cherchi provenance, author CAD reproduction, or the
paper experiment matrix. Project status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

### R55: exact geometry-proof memo for beta/angle-only state changes

The 26-handle profile identifies repeated whole-geometry audits as a measured
cost, and the paper functional separates fill/angle variables from cage-handle
geometry. A one-entry, bounded proof memo is now attached to the immutable
source-cage checker. Its identity hashes every deformed point bit-for-bit,
source/configuration identities, and all geometry-check settings. It can reuse
only a prior `CHECKS_PASSED` audit when the full optimizer state differs; each
request still reruns the source functional and refreshes its full-state input
and source-output hashes. Changed deformed geometry, incomplete checks,
resource failures, and rejected geometry do not hit this memo. The memo keeps
exact pair, subset, clearance, and region proof records; it does not weaken or
skip any check for a new geometry. Cached-stage elapsed times are zero and
explicitly mean “not re-executed.” Cache telemetry is excluded from the
scientific digest.

The actual-source regression verifies that changing beta/phi leaves all
deformed points bit-identical, causes a full geometry memo hit, refreshes the
source-output identity, avoids re-running exact pair Booleans, and produces the
same scientific digest as an independent uncached audit of that new state.
Changing a handle still enters the geometric path. On the 8-primitive fixture,
the first exact geometry proof took about `742.5 ms`; the reused geometry
stages recorded `0 ms`, and an independent recomputation took about `730.0 ms`.
This is a correctness result and a measured upper bound on the avoided work for
that small fixture, not evidence that the full 26-handle solve now fits its
budget. The geometry and guarded-SLQP native regressions both passed in the
current build (`2/2`, `51.21 s`). The frozen 26-handle case was not rerun.
Overall status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

respectively. This local evidence points to finite-difference scale
sensitivity near this hyperboloid, but does not yet pass the complete
Cherchi-output Jacobian gate or establish a solver fix. No Newton profile,
target, or tolerance was changed.

Overall status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

### R51: selector boundary is a rejected trial, not terminal cause

Reviewing the seed-1 event sequence showed that the top-level `diagnostic`
retains a failed evaluation message even when the line search subsequently
continues. At the captured near-transition trial, the closed-source selector
value was `-9.772581181788365e-12` against the existing threshold `1e-10`;
the trial was rejected. A subsequent smaller step was accepted, and the solve
continued through the remaining evaluations. The authoritative terminal
event is `maximum_evaluations_reached` at evaluation 60, so the method-level
conclusion is budget exhaustion, not selector termination. The selector
guard itself behaved as specified; no threshold or Boolean branch rule was
changed. Future result interpretation uses `solver_status` and the final
attempt event as terminal evidence, with `diagnostic` treated as the retained
evaluation diagnostic rather than a unique terminal reason.

### R53: source-motion cost decomposition and provenance-safe refactor

An opt-in local timing probe was used on the frozen seed-0 level-5
hyperboloid source, then removed from production sources. It measured about
5.7 seconds in `interface_velocity`, with 8,537 fixed-normal vertices and 414
truncation vertices. Those 414 vertices triggered 171,396 candidate-edge
checks, but a second breakdown showed the exact Sturm-Tarski certificates
accounted for `5.69544` seconds of the `5.71325`-second stage. Therefore the
edge scan is not the dominant current level-5 cost, and eliminating it is not
claimed as a runtime improvement.

The triangulator now optionally retains the exact normal-sphere edge identity
that created each hyperboloid truncation vertex. Source-motion verification
uses this construction provenance directly, while retaining checks that the
edge is a true inside/outside crossing, the root normal lies in its interior,
and the exact single-admissible-root certificate succeeds. Cap vertices keep
the sentinel identity. This metadata is excluded from the legacy geometry
hash. A reusable `TruncationRootCertifier` context also shares the exact
quadric/radius rational setup across edge proofs; each edge still runs its own
Sturm-Tarski proof.

Regression evidence: `quadric-source-motion-regression` completed with the
same pre-refactor scientific hash
`88f60a02dbb1119531d4506dad04d0bb8e13108902dd6506190fef719cd05343`, the
existing multi-step source-motion finite differences, adaptive-radius cases,
and captured level-5/level-7 cases. A reused-context certificate produced the
same acceptance and diagnostic as the single-edge wrapper. A post-change
level-5 timing remained about 5.8 seconds, confirming that this refactor is
not enough to clear the one-hour eight-restart budget. No long solver run was
repeated. Next performance work requires a separately verified reduction in
exact per-edge Sturm cost; absent that, proceed to the planned Faster
application integration rather than retrying the same expensive campaign.
Overall project status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

is justified by this experiment; continue on the planned CAD/remaining
application integration and retain the strict Cherchi→Newton limitation.

### R59: verified quadric-side witness for non-convex capped sources — 2026-09-27

The R58 log correction above is based on the saved evaluation-57/58/59 records:
the accepted evaluation-57 centroid is inside, whereas candidate-58/59
centroids are outside their closed source meshes. The candidate-58 centroid
has positive q while the independent interior samples used in the earlier
diagnostic were negative-q. This is why centroid sign alone cannot safely
select the Boolean side.

Added `quadricSolidInteriorIsPositiveRegion`. It first uses the finite-volume
centroid only when a winding query verifies that point is strictly inside the
closed outward source and q is separated from zero. Otherwise, it examines
only non-cap interface faces and compares each outward area normal with
`grad(q)`. It returns a side only if every usable interface face agrees;
degenerate, nearly tangent, missing, or conflicting witnesses fail closed.
The native evaluator and the strict Cherchi runner now consume this same side
classification. The source-motion binding exposes cap provenance and the
selection status/method for evidence; no Boolean backend, tolerance, or Newton
update rule was changed.

The test-first regression was built against the captured terminal-58 quadric
and its frozen design box. It verifies that the source centroid has q>0 but is
outside by winding, while the oriented interface consistently selects q<0
inside. A separate historical full-rank source-winding case initially exposed
that a blanket normal-vote rule is too strict (mixed interface votes); the
implementation was narrowed to the verified-interior-centroid / otherwise
unanimous-interface rule, after which that prior regression passed unchanged.
This is a bounded Boolean-side correctness fix, not proof of derivative
validity or solver convergence.

Current focused verification on `build/two-track-diag-v1-20260926`:

- `spin-faster-source-motion`, `spin-faster-quadric-moments`,
  `spin-faster-quadric-source-winding`,
  `spin-faster-quadric-source-interior-side-witness`, and
  `spin-faster-cherchi-motion-differential`: `5/5` passed.
- The strict Cherchi/Newton runner Python suite: `56/56` passed.
- The 3D OCCT and 2D/floating application results remain limited to the
  synthetic method fixtures reported in R58. The strict seed-0/1 reruns and
  their results are recorded in R60 below.

Overall status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`: the strict Cherchi/Newton solve must
still be rerun and may expose additional derivative/topology issues; Spin-It
joint deformation still lacks a successful nontrivial binary-material result;
author assets/parameters, paper-scale experiments, arbitrary-quadric CAD, and
final manufacturing/physical gates remain outstanding.

### R61: seed-1 source-side rejection classified as a real ambiguity — 2026-09-27

Added a focused regression from seed-1's first ambiguous hyperboloid trial.
This was diagnostic-only: no Boolean rule, quadric, tolerance, or solver
update was changed. The test samples inward offsets at 32 evenly spread
non-cap interface locations and checks each with winding number; all 32
certified interior points had `q>0` and winding `+1`. It also samples the 19
interface facets whose outward normals vote the opposite way: 18 yielded
winding-certified interior points with `q<0`. Across all non-cap faces, the
vote counts are `19` positive and `18,563` negative; the minority faces carry
about `0.00192202` of summed interface area. All interface vertices remain on
the analytic quadric within `2.34e-14` absolute residual.

Thus the captured capped source actually has winding-certified interior
points on both sides of the same analytic quadric. Majority voting, a centroid
fallback, or selecting one side from a single local sample would silently
choose a Boolean that does not represent the whole source. The current
`UNKNOWN_SIDE` rejection is therefore the correct fail-closed behavior for
this geometry, not a demonstrated selector implementation bug. The focused
regression now asserts that this exact mixed-side input is rejected. The
existing outside-centroid/unanimous-interface case remains separately
covered.

Verification on `build/two-track-diag-v1-20260926`: rebuilt
`quadric-moment-evaluator-regression`; CTest
`spin-faster-quadric-source-winding` and
`spin-faster-quadric-source-interior-side-witness` passed `2/2`, and
`git diff --check` passed. This classifies one rejected trial but does not
explain all of seed-1's 60-evaluation exhaustion or establish infeasibility.
No automatic side-selection heuristic is justified; the strict solver must
retain/restart around such a trial or report the budget limit. Overall status
remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

### R66: narrow exact terminal-domain predicate exposes a represented-set leak — 2026-09-27

The broad exact-region relation computes contact, overlap, both subset
directions, equality, and symmetric-difference volume atomically. On the
current Cherchi terminal mesh its optional symmetric-difference volume failed
with a non-polygon facet cycle, withholding otherwise relevant inclusion
facts. A narrow exact inclusive-subset operation was added to the native Nef
API and Python binding; it computes only the unregularized `A - B` difference
and publishes the subset result only after that exact operation succeeds.
The broader relation contract is unchanged.

The first seed-0 rerun using this predicate still converged in 19 function
evaluations, but the independent final-geometry gate correctly failed:
exact represented-set containment reports that the terminal Cherchi material
is not a subset of the represented design cube. Its binary64 AABB extrema are
approximately `[-1.0000000000000007, -1.0000000000000004, -1]` to
`[1.0000000000000002, 1.0000000000000007, 1]`; 38 material vertices lie
strictly outside the cube's AABB. The AABB count is diagnostic only; the
failure is based on the exact Nef subset predicate, not a tolerance or bbox
proxy. The terminal boundary itself and its independent moment audit had
passed earlier in the run. No coordinate snapping or tolerance was added.

The terminal audit now preserves a structured `OUTSIDE_DESIGN_DOMAIN` result
and the exact relation instead of collapsing it into a generic exception.
Direct exact-region tests and the Cherchi Newton runner tests passed `70/70`
after the API and reporting changes. The seed-0 output directory is
`build/strict-faster-cherchi-domain-audit-seed0-20260927-r2/`; its run remains
`FINAL_GEOMETRY_AUDIT_FAILED`, not a successful full method result. It is
evidence that equation convergence alone does not establish admissible
material geometry. The next solver-side task is to localize why Boolean
triangulation produces these slightly out-of-domain represented coordinates;
do not round them away without a separately justified geometry contract.
Overall status remains `PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

A second independent seed-0 process on the subsequently rebuilt binary is
preserved in `build/strict-faster-cherchi-domain-audit-seed0-20260927-r3/`.
It reproduces the same 19-evaluation convergence, trace hash, event hash,
`OUTSIDE_DESIGN_DOMAIN` exact subset result, and 38 AABB-outside diagnostic
vertices. Both current-runner terminal audits therefore reject the geometry;
the repeated result does not promote it to a successful solve.

This does not resolve the separate exact terminal-domain leak recorded in

### R68: exact-coordinate export and seed-0 terminal domain gate — 2026-09-27

The R66 seed-0 failure was traced to the strict Cherchi probe's coordinate
adapter. The pinned reference library's custom `bigrational::get_d()` is a
truncating conversion and its custom `bignatural::operator==` implementation
does not provide a safe tie test. The adapter now performs exact quotient /
remainder round-to-nearest, ties-to-even conversion itself. It only replaces a
legacy approximate coordinate when the two differ by at most eight scaled
binary64 epsilons; larger exact/approximate disagreements are retained and
labelled `APPROXIMATION_DISAGREES` in the exact-coordinate sidecar. The
ordinary non-diagnostic probe path is unchanged. Exact sidecars now follow the
canonical OBJ vertex order, and point-to-output association is checked against
the probe's approximate-coordinate keys before any replacement.

The new scale-100.3 oblique-subtraction regression reproduces the former
one-ULP outward coordinate before the fix, then verifies exact boundary
coordinates, in-domain output, deterministic output, raw moments and complete
sidecar alignment. `experiments/cherchi_reference_probe/test_probe.py` passes
with the rebuilt probe. The pinned Cherchi source itself was not modified.

The same frozen synthetic YO-YO request and deterministic seed 0 were rerun
from fresh directories `build/strict-faster-cherchi-domain-audit-seed0-20260927-r4/`
and `...-r5/`, each with 60 maximum function evaluations and one restart.

self-intersection, moment-map and Nef subset audits passed. The final exact
material is a subset of the represented design cube, with zero AABB-outside

versus sealed-faceted material gate remains unchanged at `1e-6`.

### R73: saddle CAD mismatch localized across geometry representations — 2026-09-27

Completed the prescribed same-input comparison for the reversed oblique

### R78: Joint-cage update evidence uses accepted within-pass transitions — 2026-09-27

The nontrivial source-cage acceptance test previously used a positive
`acceptedStep` anywhere in a pass as a gate, then inferred an update by
comparing the pass initial state to the terminal state. This could miss a
real accepted intermediate update if a later accepted transition returned a
block to its initial value. It also did not directly tie the claimed change
to the SLQP transition marked accepted.

The test now follows the solver trace convention: each iteration row stores
the pre-line-search state, and its `acceptedStep` marks the transition to the
next row. Fill and handle changes are therefore checked on adjacent states
within the same functional/partition. The index-guard regression covers an
accepted intermediate handle change followed by return to the initial state,
confirms that a rejected transition cannot count as an update, and rejects a
trace whose initial state does not match the pass input. The scientific solver
and its objective, constraints, and line search were not changed. This repairs
test evidence only; it does not prove the 26-handle adaptive run reaches a
binary-material endpoint.

Verification on 2026-09-27:

- Test-first regressions failed before helper implementation for both
  accepted-intermediate and mismatched-initial-state cases (exit 1).
- Rebuilt `spin-it-2014-source-cage-binary-material-regression` in the pinned
  `build/strict-v2-spin` configuration with `-j4` — passed.
- Focused binary-material and joint-index guard CTests — **2/2 passed**;
  the final binary-material regression completed in 54.10 s (the earlier
  53.26 s run preceded the mismatched-initial-state guard).
- Strict Faster nonpositive-initial-multiplier and nonpositive-stationary
  restart CTests — **2/2 passed**.
- The 26-handle `--joint-material` experiment was not run; the existing
  geometry-guard cost/budget evidence still makes an unchanged full run
  unjustified.

This closes the test-side terminal-index/accepted-transition evidence defect,
not the joint-deformation convergence gate. Overall status remains
`PARTIAL_BLOCKED`.

### R84–R85: fixed the multi-restart terminal-evidence binding — 2026-09-27

Expanded the answer-independent seed-1 diagnostic to permit up to eight
restarts (60 function evaluations per attempt). R84 reached the solver's
convergence condition on restart 5 after 203 evaluations; restart budgets were
`[60, 60, 60, 1, 22]`, the positive multiplier was `2.706740578893841`, and
the normalized Eq. 14 residual was `1.1570876411233764e-13`. Its external
terminal geometry audit nevertheless failed with
`terminal trace and material quadric identity differ`.

The failure was an audit-index bug, not an optimizer or Boolean failure. The
native trace's `function_evaluations` counter is local to its restart (22 at
this endpoint), while evaluation records use a run-global index (203 at the
same endpoint). The old audit joined these unrelated counters and selected
the earlier global evaluation 22. Added a regression with a prior-restart
evaluation whose global index equals the terminal restart-local counter; it
failed against the old implementation, then passed after the audit joined by
quadric, geometry, and topology identities instead. The fix does not change
solver settings, trial acceptance, or material geometry.

Re-ran the same seed-1 profile as R85 against the corrected audit. It again
converged after the same 203 evaluations and restart budgets, with the same
trace hash, final quadric, represented geometry, and terminal material SHA-256
`1270ec0d62be9f2625a8df25ae312670667de238c3efe57dbd8117c3f6bcaf06`. The
external audit now passes: exact boundary audit, independent mass-moment
comparison (normalized infinity residual `1.8041124150158794e-16`), and exact
represented material-subset check all passed. Freshness is `CURRENT`.

A follow-up metadata review found the audit summary labeled the local count
`22` as `terminal_solver_evaluation_index`; the selected evaluation's actual
run-global solver index is `203`. The checker now reports both values under
separate field names and validates the global index. Re-running only the
terminal audit on the immutable R85 result and mesh passed with the corrected
fields; the original `result.json` was not edited. The supplemental record is
`build/strict-cherchi-answer-independent-seed1-r8-20260927-r85/terminal-audit-recheck.json`.

R84/R85's raw native event hashes differ because 19 rejected-trial diagnostic
strings include Python traceback line numbers shifted by the audit edit; all
per-evaluation quadric, geometry, topology, map, source, cap, and source-side
selector identities match. Added a separate canonical event digest that
normalizes only Python traceback frame line numbers while retaining the raw
events and native hash unchanged. Applying it to both saved event sequences
produces the same digest
`dc11c7dd54c69c7fdcb7a121d545d09a3579c3f168cda75b37114cc7203c9bd4`.
Thus the scientific trace, terminal geometry, and canonicalized event meaning
repeat exactly; the raw full-event hashes remain different and are retained as
such. New runner outputs carry both hashes under result schema v5. The
comparison record is
`build/strict-cherchi-answer-independent-seed1-r8-20260927-r85/canonical-event-repeat-comparison.json`.
The current Cherchi result is a successful synthetic method validation only;
it is not an author-paper case. The focused runner test file passed **59/59**

### R87: separated stable event identity from raw traceback evidence — 2026-09-27

Added `_canonical_event_digest` and result schema v5. It normalizes only
Python traceback frame line numbers inside traceback-bearing strings before
canonical JSON hashing. The original native `event_hash` and the complete raw
events remain unchanged and are still serialized. A test was written and
observed failing before the helper existed, then passed after implementation;
the test also proves that changing the actual failure message changes the
canonical digest and that hashing does not mutate the input evidence.

Applied the helper to the preserved R84/R85 event arrays. Their raw native
event hashes remain different, while both produce canonical digest
`dc11c7dd54c69c7fdcb7a121d545d09a3579c3f168cda75b37114cc7203c9bd4` and have
the same native trace hash. The comparison record binds both source result
SHA-256 values and is
`build/strict-cherchi-answer-independent-seed1-r8-20260927-r85/canonical-event-repeat-comparison.json`.
No solver was rerun: this is a deterministic re-analysis of immutable event
ledgers, not a new optimization result. Future run outputs will include both
the raw and canonical hashes; this does not make the raw hashes equal.

Ruling: retain both hashes and treat the canonical digest as an additional
semantic comparison, not as a replacement for byte-exact event evidence —
line numbers can shift without changing event meaning, while retaining the raw
hash preserves the ability to detect any such change — cost if wrong: a
line-number-only distinction might matter to an external audit protocol that
requires byte identity. The focused runner suite passed **59/59**.
`VERIFIED_COMPUTATIONAL_REPRODUCTION` remains unmet.

### R92: bounded exact primitive-region reuse for changing cage geometry — 2026-09-27

Implemented a bounded FIFO memo for `ExactMaterialRegion` objects keyed by the

complexity; its configured limits are included in adaptive, rebind, binary-
material, and guarded-SLQP configuration identities. Zero caps disable it.
Only the primitive region construction can be reused. For every current state,
the checker still performs fresh exact subset against the current outer region,
fresh outer-domain clearance, and all required pair-overlap checks. No result
from a changed outer geometry is reused as a containment or clearance proof.

and changed primitive boundaries, disabled caching, one-entry eviction, and
independent uncached proof-row equality. The geometry regression passed on
three consecutive implementations of this final change (**15.69 s**,
**15.67 s**, and **16.38 s** after the final cache-validity guard);
source-cage binary-material, adaptive
solver, and adaptive-rebind regressions passed (**53.04 s**, **21.00 s**, and
**10.43 s**) on the current build. `git diff --check` was clean. A broad CTest
selector initially included an adaptive-rebind executable that had not yet
been built (`Not Run`); it was then explicitly built and that test passed.

On the small eight-primitive fixture, one primitive-region hit and seven
misses reduced the measured primitive-region stage sum from **309.41 ms** on an

原始F/h/D多项式。12种重新计算摘要后的错误证据被拒绝。输入绑定反例还
发现v1检查器的范围缺口：保留其源码/历史回执，新增v2独立检查补上该门；
只重审已有几何，没有为检查器改动重复优化。

真实RED为缺少功能的运行失败。后续编译头文件遗漏及环形夹具的上下弧
选取歧义均保留日志；后者按已固定的正y间隙及实际横坐标范围纠正，没有
改变模型、插值比例、几何守卫或容差。最终归档
`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-05-rank-two-complete-source-implementation/r114-final-verification.json`
核验11份终态回执、409项当前输入、2374项历史源快照和50份检查器文件。

**边界仍然明确：**本批关闭移动内部节点的构造和上述方法范围内的独立验证，
不授予公共网格插入、全部三角形/内部边严格厚度、全局兼容同胚、2^-20源
精度、闭合3D/cap、10×8运动导数或论文完整复现。一点有正厚度不意味着
全部旧三角形已摆脱零厚度。下一步将实际节点接入公共载体并重三角化，
检查真实面与边；不删除失败区域。Task3仍为IN_PROGRESS，Tasks4–8待做。
未执行全项目必需测试，未提交/打标签/合并/替换共享库，整体仍为
`PARTIAL_BLOCKED`，长期目标ACTIVE。

## R115：移动节点已进入实际公共三角网格，厚度门仍未关闭

2026-10-06，在已批准的秩二完整源计划下串行推进Task3，保留旧网格和
全部失败记录。新增私有`RankTwoInteriorMesh`：使用同一覆盖域和节点
权威，对实际命中的三角形或共享内部边做精确局部细分。原来的约束
三角化、区域标签、外环和孔洞不变；内部边两侧共同细分，不删除失败面。

两次独立构建/进程实际通过已注册测试，耗时154.981235415s与
156.048074560s；412项有效输入相同，109604928字节的科学输出完全一致。
盘面实际为25点/24三角形；圆环为2183点/2856三角形，保留3个原生区域
孔洞及Empty/Cut/Full的1/2/1标签。独立保存结果检查核验4个节点的8条
插值关系、26个截面根、输入绑定、局部细分和最终拓扑；不调用原生工厂。
10种重新计算哈希后的科学破坏记录全部被拒绝。

本次也明确复现了尚未解决的厚度问题：中心节点使3个盘面三角形具有
正的内部厚度，但这3个面都仍连接零厚度内部边；另有21个零厚度面和
21条零厚度内部边。严格通过面数仍为0，不能宣布完整材料通过。测试
最初把“有正厚度角点”误当作完整条件，真实失败后按原面/边双重规则
纠正分类，没有放宽守卫。独立v2检查额外证明24个原边界点的圆方程；
实际(0,0)内部点不能冒充零厚度边界。圆环全量厚度尚未检查。

归档：`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-05-rank-two-complete-source-implementation/r115-final-verification.json`，
核验9份终态回执、412项当前输入、1594项历史快照和55份检查器文件。
旧v1检查器和历史证据保留。仅本批受影响测试通过，全项目必需测试未跑。

**下一步：**为实际零厚度内部边构造带来源依赖的移动点，共同细分并
重查所有活跃面与边。全局兼容同胚、2^-20源精度、闭合3D/cap、真实
10×8运动导数、Cherchi集成和完整矩阵仍是后续门，不由本批转授。
Task3仍在进行，Tasks4–8待做；未提交/打标签/合并/替换共享库，整体
仍为`PARTIAL_BLOCKED`，长期目标ACTIVE。

## R116：实际内部共享边细分，圆盘严格厚度通过（2026-10-06）

新增不可变移动边节点工厂，使用实际父网格、两个端点来源及固定有理
权重构造精确坐标。不是根隔离区间中点，也不是伪造原生弧采样。
工厂验证真实双侧同区域归属、排除原约束边并重算原始材料符号；
反向端点与互补权重规范化为同一身份。实际细分同时替换内部边两侧
面片，重新核验正向子面、原边界、全部点、连通分量及Euler孔洞。

**新增方法结果：**R115圆盘的21条零厚度内部边已全部插入移动点；
实际46点、66三角形、87条内部边，全部面与内部边通过原严格厚度
内核。原24条边界保留，没有删除零厚度区域，也没有替换为任意扇形。

当前版本两次独立构建/进程均实际通过注册测试，耗时70.588555181s、
70.877471007s；415项有效输入一致，625821字节科学输出完全一致。
两份保存结果分别经独立检查：42条坐标插值关系、24个原边界圆方程、
原多项式符号、每步双侧真实面片、正向子面与最终拓扑/厚度重新核验。
12种重新计算哈希后的科学破坏被拒绝，其中4种同时更新内层节点摘要。

当前旧圆盘/圆环回归也通过，整个R115科学输出保持不变；它不授予
圆环全量厚度通过。归档位于
`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-05-rank-two-complete-source-implementation/r116-final-verification.json`，
核验11份终态回执、415项当前输入、2410项历史快照和58份检查器文件。

**范围与下一步：**这里只关闭冻结圆盘的严格面/内部边厚度门。
接下来从原生区域/弧归属推导实际上下提升及真正材料边界，检查圆环
全部活跃区域与内部边。全局兼容同胚、2^-20源精度、全部法向面共同
载体、闭合3D/cap、真实10×8运动、Cherchi/Python集成和完整矩阵仍待做。
Task3继续进行，Tasks4–8待做；全项目必需测试未跑，未提交/打标签/
合并/替换共享库，整体仍为`PARTIAL_BLOCKED`，长期目标ACTIVE。
